# Changelog

All notable changes to `precision-soft/melody/v3` will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

The example application inside this major keeps no changelog: it is not a project with a past, it has one state — the present one — and [`.example/README.md`](.example/README.md) is the whole of its documentation.

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- http/middleware: `(*FixedWindowLimiter).SetEvictOldestKeyAtCeiling` and its sliding twin decide what a full table answers an unseen key: on, the key whose last request is the oldest gives up its bucket, so a flood of unseen keys costs an idle caller its count rather than every stranger its admission, and a ceiling lowered under a full table evicts the oldest keys at once; off, the default, the unseen key is denied as before.
- messagebus: `RegisterTransports` registers a `TransportsCloser` beside the transports map, under `ServiceTransportsCloser`, resolved as a dependency of the map; `(*ConsumeCommand).IsLongRunning` has the application journal the unbounded default cache backend when a consumer is dispatched from the console, as for an http process, and a command of the application's own that runs until stopped declares the same method. CACHE.md says so.
- exception: `PanicCause` reads a recovered panic value as the cause of the error a recovery boundary fabricates in its place, a typed nil answering no cause, since its `Error()` would dereference a nil receiver; `Logged` answers an error that reports itself already logged, for a writer that filed its record, and `IsAlreadyLogged` reads the mark at the depth `MarkLogged` writes it.
- openapi: **Behavioural change**: `Registry.MarkServing` records that the wiring phase is over; `Application.Run` calls it when it marks the configuration serving, and a `Describe` issued after it is refused at the door, naming the route.
- http: `DeleteCookieWithDomain(response, name, path, domain)` writes the expiring counterpart of a cookie set with a `Domain`, which `DeleteCookie` writes host-only and the browser therefore never matches; a `__Host-` name drops the domain its prefix forbids.
- http: `ServerSentEventHub.ClearBackplaneWithContext(ctx)` clears the backplane and waits for the broadcasts holding it under the caller's deadline, answering the context's error when the deadline ended the wait; the amqp backplane's `CloseWithContext` clears itself through it.
- container: `WithCloser[T](closer func(context.Context, T) error)` closes a service through the closer, handed the teardown's remaining budget, in place of the `Close` and `CloseWithContext` doors of a value whose type the application does not own; the service keeps its type. It covers the built value, an evicted override, a value built after closing began and an override of the closer's type; another type closes through its own doors. `RegisterOptions.Closer` and `RegisterOptions.CloserValueType` carry it; `ErrCloserTypeMismatch` refuses a closer the provider's type is not assignable to, `ErrScopedCloserUnsupported` the option on both scoped registrations. The amqp connection service uses it.
- container: `WithoutTeardownReflection()` keeps the armed teardown's walk out of a service's memory, for a service whose own goroutines rewrite a pointer field, which was a data race under `go test -race` at its filing and at `ArmParallelTeardown`: its value is not walked under its name, its type or an override of the name, and a walk reaching it records the edge without entering it. `RegisterOptions` gains `SkipsTeardownReflection`.
- container: `ContextCloser` names, on the contract package, the door the teardown prefers on every value that carries it — `CloseWithContext(context.Context) error`, Close under a deadline the caller declares — asked by a type assertion, never a method of `Container` or a service contract; the container, the request scope, the application's close, the server-sent-event hub and the message bus ask for it by this one name
- container: `ArmParallelTeardown() error` on the concrete container, reached by type assertion as `IsClosed` and `CloseWithContext` are, closes in one wave every service the dependency graph proves unrelated to the others in it, each with the whole remaining budget, so a component after a slow one is no longer reached with the deadline gone; off unless armed. Arm it after the wiring and before the run, the orderings written by a provider that resolves its collaborator or by `WithTeardownDependency`: an edge naming a service never registered is refused with `ErrTeardownDependencyWasNeverRegistered`, and two `Close` methods sharing an external state (a file, a table, a `sql.Register` name) only a declaration holds apart. `ErrTeardownDependencyIsScoped` is the cause `ArmParallelTeardown`, and any registration after it, answers for a declared dependency naming a scoped service
- container: `WithTeardownDependencyOfType[T]()` declares a teardown ordering keyed by the collaborator's TYPE, beside the form keyed by its name, for a collaborator reached through `GetByType` or whose name belongs to a module the declaring code does not own; `T` and `*T` name one node. A registration that does not file its type may declare on its own Go type; only one that files the type it declares against is refused as declaring on itself.
- container: `container.ErrScopeClosed` and `container.ErrContainerClosed` are the causes every closed-lifetime refusal carries — ten scope refusals, the lazy handle's and the container's eight — so `errors.Is` tells a request that ended from an application shutting down without matching the message.
- container: `container.WithTeardownDependency(serviceNames ...string)` declares that a registration must be CLOSED BEFORE the services it names, without resolving them; an empty name and a self-reference are refused at registration with `ErrTeardownDependencyNameIsRequired` and `ErrTeardownDependencyIsSelf`, and `RegisterScoped` refuses it with `ErrScopedTeardownDependencyUnsupported`, a scope building its graph from its own resolutions; `DeclareTeardownDependency(serviceName, dependencyNames...)`, on the concrete container by type assertion like `ArmParallelTeardown`, declares the edge after the registration with the rules of `WithTeardownDependency`, for an author who is not the one who knows the edge.
- container: a service the container owns stays one instance for the process even when resolved through a request scope; `Replacing()` admits a scoped registration whose name or registered type a singleton already claims, not the reverse; `ClosedWithScope()` hands an installed override to the scope's teardown, through the new `OverrideInstanceWithOptions` and `OverrideProtectedInstanceWithOptions`; a scope closes what it built in dependency order, dependents before their dependencies, falling back to creation order, latest first, and reports a cycle as the container's teardown does; a collection gathered on a scope holds the scope's registrations and the container's, one gathered by a container provider only the container's.
- container: `IsClosed` reports whether a `Close` already began tearing the container down; `ServiceDescriptions` on the built container answers what it can say WITHOUT running a provider
- cache: `cache.DeserializationError` is the typed error a read answers for a stored payload that does not deserialize, with `NewDeserializationError` and `IsDeserializationError` as its doors; `cache.NewManagerOwningBackend` builds the manager whose `Close` cascades into the backend, the cascade `NewManager` no longer performs; `(*Manager).NormalizeStoredValue` answers the shape a value stored through this manager reads back as, one local serializer round-trip.
- cache: `RememberOption.WithContext` ties a caller's wait to the request that opened it, and `Context` reads it back; it answers a COPY of the option carrying the context and leaves the receiver as it was, the zero value included, so a service keeps one configured option and derives a per-request one at every call, where written into the shared receiver, as the frozen majors' `WithContext` does, one request's context governed every concurrent wait on the option. A caller that discards the answered value keeps the option without a context.
- cache: `cache/contract.StoredValueNormalizer` names on the contract package the door through which `Remember` learns the shape a stored value reads back as, `NormalizeStoredValue(value any) (any, error)`, which `Manager` implements; it is asked of the `Cache` value `Remember` was handed, so a decorator over the manager implements it too, forwarding to the decorated manager, or the miss and the hit answer two shapes again; it was an unexported interface of the `cache` package.
- session: `session.ErrSessionDeleted` is the cause the save refusal carries for a session deleted while the request holding it ran, and `ErrSessionRotated` names one refused because the id was rotated away, beside `ErrSessionDeleted`: a logout expires the browser cookie, a rotation moves the identity to the fresh id the rotating request hands the client
- session: `session.NewManagerWithClock(storage, ttl, tombstoneRetention, clock)` reads every instant of the tombstone record from the given clock, and `NewInMemoryStorageWithClock`, `NewFileStorageFromPathWithClock` and `NewFileStorageFromFileWithClock` read every expiry instant, stored, compared and the sweep's tick, from an injected clock; the framework-wired default storage runs on the kernel's.
- session: `session.NewManagerWithTombstoneRetention` builds a manager whose write-back refusal window the caller sizes, refusing a non-positive window with a panic naming the rule, `session.TombstoneRetention` being the default; `session.NewManagerOwningStorage` is the constructor whose `Close` ends the storage too, the cascade `NewManager` no longer performs.
- logging: **Behavioural change**: `NewReopenableFileWriter` opens the journal behind a writer that can be told to open its path again, and the container-built logger arms it on `SIGHUP` in every process, the console commands included, so a `postrotate` `SIGHUP` rotates a long-running worker's journal too. `SIGHUP` no longer terminates a process whose journal is open, a console command whose terminal hung up included: stop one with `SIGTERM` or `SIGINT`.
- logging: `logging.ErrRotatedDescriptorNotClosed` is the sentinel `ReopenableFileWriter.Reopen` joins to the one failure after a successful rotation; `logging/contract.LevelReporter` is the optional capability by which a `Logger` says whether a record at a level would survive its threshold, `logging.LevelEnabled` the door onto it; `NewJsonLoggerWithClock` stamps records from an injected clock, the kernel's for the container-built logger; `Closed()` on the json logger reports whether `Close` really closed the underlying writer, which `NewRequestLogger`'s decorator forwards
- logging: `NewStandardErrorLogger` adapts a melody logger to the `*log.Logger` the standard library asks for, so net/http's `Server.ErrorLog` or bun's diagnostic channel reaches the application's journal instead of stderr; `NewProcessLogger` is the console sibling of `NewRequestLogger`, the cli entry point decorating with it.
- logging: `RunShieldedStep` runs one step under the exit handler's own budget and answers whether it finished, so the normal return of `Run` tears down under the panic path's shield; `LogOnRecoverAndExitAfter` logs the recovered value as `LogOnRecoverAndExit` does and runs a `beforeExit` hook between the record and the exit, under the budget its caller declares, a non-positive one taking the exit handler's own.
- documentation: `README.md` carries one version table — each line's first release, latest tag, status and the event that ends its fixes — which `SECURITY.md` links to instead of a second, drifted one, with v4 planned for Q1 2027 and v3's own boundary; `README.md` and `.documentation/UPGRADE.md` date the v4 cut at Q1 2027 and say what it is for: adopt each replacement while still on v3 and the cut costs nothing. The frozen majors keep the undated wording, their support bounded by the event
- validation: `ValidatorLimits`, `DefaultValidatorLimits` and `NewValidatorWithLimits` configure the bounds of one `Validate` answer: its entries (1,000 by default), the bytes of the path one entry spells (256) and the bytes of its paths and messages together (512 KiB); a validator registered over the framework's applies them to every request body, a zero field keeps its default and a negative one is refused.
- validation: `IsRuleWiringErrorCode` reports whether a code names a mistake in the DECLARATION rather than the value — an unknown rule, an unreadable tag, a pattern that does not compile — which no client input can produce, `ValidationErrors.HasRuleWiringError` asks it of a collection and `ValidationErrors.WithoutRuleWiringContext` strips the internal context of those entries for the client; `ValidationErrors` implements `json.Marshaler` as the array it is, so a log record says what the response body says
- application: two boot warnings name a resource melody supplied that nothing will ever reclaim; what `net/http` reports on its own reaches the application's journal; the comment on `MiddlewarePriorityStatic` describes where the static file server sits.
- debug: `debug.NewEventCommand` takes a declaration channel, `debug.DeferredListener` entries naming what the SERVING process wires and this process does not, so `debug:events` run from a console answers "is access control wired?" with a declaration instead of an absence.
- debug: the error-context walk stops at a depth bound; `debug:container --build` and `debug:middleware --build` are the doors that run providers and factories, and `debug.MiddlewareDescriptionProvider` and `debug.MiddlewareBuildProvider` are the two channels `debug:middleware` reads, the pipeline as selected and ordered and the chain built behind `--build`, which `NewMiddlewareCommandWithProviders` takes; `debug:router` reports `priority` and `order`, and under `--verbose` `requirements`, `defaults` and `attributes`.
- httpclient: `HttpClientConfig.WithoutRedirects() *HttpClientConfig` builds a client that hands a redirect back as the response it is, the 3xx status and its `Location` reaching the caller, and `FollowsRedirects() bool` reads the policy back: a following client re-sends a `POST` answered 301, 302 or 303 as a `GET` without its body, so a sink's success read off the redirect was another page's. The credential-stripping policy stays the default.
- httpclient: `HttpClient.Close` releases the idle connections the client's own transport holds; `RequestStreamWithContext` is `RequestStream` bound to a context, whose cancellation ends the request and the body read of a stream a server never ends.
- cli: `cli.DispatchCommand(ctx, command, runtime, arguments, writer)` parses one command line against a command's own flags and runs it with no banner, no scope close and no exit handling; a command answering the same `Flags()` instances on every call cannot be dispatched twice at once.
- cli: `--format=json-pretty` renders the envelope indented for a person, and `output.IsJsonFormat` answers for both json spellings wherever `FormatJson` was compared; `NormalizeVerbosityArguments` rewrites `-v`, `-vv` and `-vvv` into the verbosity level, the one door the console entry and `DispatchCommand` share.
- event: `event.RequiredListenerSkippedError` is the type a dispatch returns when a listener ended it, by stopping propagation or failing, with a listener marked required through `RequiredListenerRegistrar` still behind it, and `event/contract.RegisteredListener` carries `Required` and `MaySkipRequiredListeners`, telling an armed fail-closed guarantee from an unarmed one
- security: `MarkInfrastructureFailure` and `IsInfrastructureFailure` export the mark that tells a platform that could not answer from a credential that failed its checks: an application's `TokenValidator`, `TokenEnricher` or impersonation resolver returns its store outage through `MarkInfrastructureFailure`, and the bearer, internal-auth and switch-user sources file it at Error instead of the routine Info, a branch only the framework's own validators reached.
- security: `NewTwoFactorRejectedToken` and the optional `securitycontract.TwoFactorRejection` door tell a second factor supplied and refused from a challenge not yet answered: `TotpSecondFactorAuthenticator` answers a wrong or replayed code, and a recovery code the store refused, with the rejected form (a store without `TwoFactorRecoveryStore` ignores the recovery header and answers the plain challenge), and `(*TwoFactorPendingToken).SecondFactorRejected()` reads it; both stay the pending token the application prompts from.
- security: `(*PathPrefixMatcher).Prefix()` answers the raw prefix the firewall matcher compares with, and `(*PathPrefixMatcher).String()` describes it as `path prefix "/admin"`, so `CompiledFirewall.MatcherDescription()` and `SecurityContext.MatchedFirewallMatcher()` name it where they answered the empty string.
- security: `HmacTokenSourceConfig.AcceptUntypedEnvelopes` opens a migration window in which an internal-auth envelope carrying no `typ` is accepted, so a rolling upgrade does not refuse the peers not yet redeployed; default false, and a `typ` present and wrong is refused either way. Set it on the verifiers first, roll the signers, then clear it. The window is removed in v4
- security: `security/accesscontrol` is where the access control vocabulary now lives: `NewExactRule`, `NewSegmentPrefixRule`, `NewRawPrefixRule` and `NewRegexRule` each name their reach; `NewRule(path, matching, config)` takes the mode as a value and refuses the zero value; attributes arrive in a `RuleConfig` struct, so a later dimension is a field on a call site that already compiles. `security.AccessControlRule` and `security.AccessControl` alias the moved types, so existing code keeps compiling
- security: `accesscontrol.Control.MatchRule(path)` answers the rule that claims a path with its declaration index, resolved as `Match` resolves, without copying the rule list `Rules()` copies; the access control listener reads it.
- security: a revocation stops being an enumeration: `security/contract.RevocationEpochStore` declares `RevokeBefore(userIdentifier, deviceIdentifier, instant)` and `RevocationEpoch(...)`, and `EpochRevocableTokenStore` composes it with `RevocableTokenStore`, which is untouched, so a store written against an earlier release still compiles; both stores melody ships satisfy it.
- security: `securityconfig.(*Builder).BootWarnings()` answers the hazards the last `BuildAndCompile` found in the declaration, each a `securityconfig.BootWarning` with a stable `Name`, a `Message` and a `Context` that names the configuration and never a secret; the application writes them into the configured journal once, at the http boot.
- http: `(*ServerSentEventWriter).WithWriteBudget(time.Duration) *ServerSentEventWriter` re-arms the connection's write deadline before every frame, through a `ResponseController` reaching it through any wrapper, where `net/http` armed the server's `WriteTimeout` once from the request line and cut a longer stream silently: a stream that keeps writing lives, a client that stops reading is cut one budget after the socket stops buffering, zero keeps the server's deadline, and a writer the controller cannot reach writes every frame as before. A handler hands in the server's `WriteTimeout`, read off `request.Context().Value(nethttp.ServerContextKey)`, and keeps a keepalive ticker.
- http: `ServerSentEventHub.SetLogger` gives the hub the journal it never had; `ServerSentEventHub.Close` is `Shutdown` under the one name the framework's teardown recognises; `ServerSentEventHub.IsClosed` answers the difference a caller's range cannot see, and `ServerSentEventSubscriber.Topic` reports the topic a subscriber holds
- http: `FilterRouteManifestByZone` narrows a manifest to one zone and answers `(RouteManifest, error)`, the zone read as the command reads its `--zone` flag, trimmed: an empty zone answers the manifest whole and an undeclared one is refused naming the declared zones, where a misspelled zone answered an empty manifest in silence; `RouteZones` and `IsRouteZone` name the declared zones, for a command to list the accepted spellings in its refusal; `RouteManifestEntry` carries `host`, `schemes`, `locales` and `priority`.
- http: `SessionCookiePolicy.Secure` takes a `SessionCookieSecurePolicy`: the default `SessionCookieSecureFromScheme` derives the attribute from the resolved scheme as before, `SessionCookieSecureAlways` forces it on behind a proxy that terminates TLS but is not trusted for `X-Forwarded-Proto`, which emitted every session cookie without `Secure`, and `SessionCookieSecureNever` is the explicit, discouraged opt-out.
- http: a middleware reference is satisfiable when the registrations sharing the referenced name cover the referrer between them, and the reference-gating check weighs only what the group being built assembles; `PrefersHtml` is covered again for an html type that arrives alone in mixed case.
- http: **Behavioural change**: `RequestPathAsRouted(escapedPath string) string` is the spelling of a request path the router reads — the escaped path split on the separators the client sent, each segment unescaped on its own, an encoded separator kept as `%2F` inside its segment — for every other consumer of the path to read the same: the kernel's canonical-path guard, the access-control matcher and the firewall path matcher read it now, where they read the decoded `URL.Path`
- container, debug: `TeardownPlanEntry` carries `Aliases`, the other node keys of the same instance, and `Cycle`, set on a service ON a ring the drain could not open; `debug:container` answers a folded name with its node's entry, named by the `node` json key and printed `service:a (also service:b)`, a type alias as `type:*pkg.T`, shows the row on a window on either name and reads `cycle` as a ring member's `ordering`, where an alias lost its `teardown` key and a ring rendered as two `proved` rows
- container, debug: `TeardownPlan() []containercontract.TeardownPlanEntry` on the concrete container, reached by type assertion as `IsClosed` is, beside `TeardownRunsInWaves() bool`, is the read-only teardown plan, which `debug:container` renders as the `TEARDOWN (SEQUENTIAL)` / `TEARDOWN (DEPENDENCY WAVES)` block — per built service its wave, what it closes before and after, whether anything orders it — on the listing, the `--build` sweep and the single-service door, the json carrying a `teardown` object per built listed service, omitted where the plan lists none, so `jq '.data.items[] | select(.teardown.ordering == "none")'` names the services nothing orders
- container, http, messagebus, logging: the teardown budget an operator declares REACHES the services released: `CloseWithContext(ctx context.Context) error` is the optional door the container's teardown prefers, falling back to `Close() error`, the `Container` contract untouched; `http.ServerSentEventHub` and `messagebus.TransportsCloser` carry it, as do the amqp transport and backplane, the bunorm registry and the audit storage. The deadline is SHARED, and the deadline record names the service that ate it and those left with none; a clean `SIGINT` under a stalled broker exits 1 naming them. `MELODY_TEARDOWN_TIMEOUT=0` runs the teardown complete.
- application, config, logging: `MELODY_TEARDOWN_TIMEOUT` (`kernel.teardown_timeout`) declares how long a clean shutdown may spend releasing what it holds, spent by the shield `logging.RunShieldedStepWithin`, where a package constant of ten seconds no deployment could change ruled it and a clean `SIGINT` exited 1 with one line on standard error. **Zero means no deadline**; a negative value fails the boot. It reaches the clean return from `Run` and the exit handler a panic or an `exception.Exit` takes, which, as the clean return does, reaches the brokers, pools and tracer providers a `Close` releases, keeping the package constant only when its caller declares none.



- documentation: `SECURITY.md` teaches the access control rules through `security/accesscontrol`, names the four reaches beside the constructor that builds each, and points the type and method links at the package that declares them

- config: four keys reach their consumer through `config/contract.ExtendedHttpConfiguration`, a sibling of `HttpConfiguration`, a configuration without it taking the default: `MELODY_STATIC_EXCLUDED_PATHS` (`kernel.static.excluded_paths`, read through `config/contract.ExtendedHttpConfiguration.StaticExcludedPaths`) names, comma separated, the path prefixes the built-in file server declines without touching the disk, none by default; `MELODY_HTTP_SESSION_TTL` (`kernel.http.session_ttl`, `config/contract.ExtendedHttpConfiguration.SessionTtl`) how long a stored session stays valid, default `config.DefaultSessionTtl`; `MELODY_HTTP_SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT` (`kernel.http.shutdown_timeout`, `config/contract.ExtendedHttpConfiguration.ShutdownTimeout`) how long a stopping http server waits for the requests it admitted before cutting them, default `config.DefaultHttpShutdownTimeout`; `MELODY_HTTP_SESSION_TOMBSTONE_RETENTION` (`kernel.http.session_tombstone_retention`, `config/contract.ExtendedHttpConfiguration.SessionTombstoneRetention`) how long a deleted session id refuses a write-back, default `config.DefaultSessionTombstoneRetention`, five minutes. **C→v4**: each method joins `config/contract.HttpConfiguration`.
- event: `event/contract.SubscriberRegistration` identifies one installation of a subscriber, which `event/contract.SubscriberRegistrar.AddSubscriberWithRegistration` answers and `RemoveSubscriberRegistration` removes; both framework dispatchers implement the registrar. **C→v4**: `AddSubscriber` answers the registration and `RemoveSubscriber` takes it.
- http: `RegenerateRequestSession` rotates the session id of a request and republishes the rotated session in one call, the session-fixation defence a login handler needs, which writes the authenticated identity to the session it returns; it goes through the manager's `session/contract.SessionRegenerator` door, and a manager without it is refused with an error, the request keeping its session. `session.Manager.RegenerateSession` stays the storage-level primitive: a fresh id, the values carried over, the old entry removed and the session passed in latched out of use, so a caller that does not publish the rotated one logs the client out cleanly. **C→v4**: `RegenerateSession` joins `session/contract.Manager`.
- container: a scope is a registrar in its own right, layered over its container: `container/contract.ScopedRegistrar` declares `RegisterScoped` and `MustRegisterScoped`, which the framework container and every scope implement and `Application.RegisterScoped` asserts on the kernel's service container. `RegisterScoped` on the container declares a service whose lifetime is one scope — an http request, a message delivery, a cron tick — built on its first resolution through a scope and closed with it, through the typed `container.RegisterScoped[T]`, `MustRegisterScoped[T]`, `RegisterScopedType[T]` and `MustRegisterScopedType[T]`; on a scope it adds one to that live scope. **C→v4**: `container/contract.ScopeManager` and `container/contract.Scope` gain the two methods.
- http: `(*http.Kernel).SetMethodPolicy` installs the method policy the kernel reads on every request, whether HEAD falls back to the GET route and an unrouted OPTIONS is answered with the computed Allow header, and `MethodPolicy` moves onto the http contract; `http/contract.MethodPolicySetter` is the optional door a kernel is asserted for. **C→v4**: the method joins `http/contract.Kernel`.
- session: `(*Session).Snapshot` reads the values, the modified flag and the cleared flag under one lock acquisition, declared by `session/contract.SnapshotSession`; `session.Snapshot`, through which the response path and `SaveSession` read a session, uses it or reads `All`, `IsModified` and `IsCleared`. **C→v4**: the method joins `session/contract.Session`.
- wiring: `melody:wiring:generate` accepts `--tags`, the comma-separated build tags of the target binary, so a constructor gated on `//go:build <tag>` is scanned instead of silently dropped; `wiring.ScanWithBuildTags` takes them beside `Scan`'s two parameters, `Scan` keeping its released signature. **C→v4**: `Scan` takes the build tags as a third parameter.

- internal: `CopyAnyValue` copies one value at the depth `CopyAnyMap` copies a whole map, so a caller holding a single `any` no longer has to wrap it in a map to get the same documented boundary


- tooling: `.dev/validate/documentation.sh` checks the package documents against the code of every major, and now runs in the staged mode as well as the full one

- security: `JwtConfig.RevocationEpochSkew` widens a revocation boundary by the worst clock skew between the node that mints and the node that revokes, and covers the imprecision of `iat` itself.
- security: **Behavioural change**: `security/contract.Claims` carries `IssuedAt` and `DeviceIdentifier`.
- security: `NewJwtTokenValidatorWithRevocationEpoch` makes a JSON web token revocable, which it has never been

- http: `SimpleRateLimitWithResolver`, `IpRateLimitWithResolver` and `UserRateLimitWithResolver` take the client-ip resolver the convenience helpers could not reach.
- http: `static.FileServerConfig.SetAllowedDotPrefixList` names the dot-prefixed first path elements the file server may retrieve.

- cache: `Manager.GetCounter` reads a key written by `Increment` or `Decrement`.
- config: a dollar in an `.env` value opens a reference only where godotenv says it does — upper case, digits and underscore.
- openapi: a field the validator rejects for every value is advertised as `not: {}` rather than as an object-level contradiction.

- application: `ScopedServiceModule` is the module hook for request-lifetime services, with `RegisterScopedServices(registrar ScopedServiceRegistrar)` running at boot beside `RegisterServices`.
- wiring: `//melody:scoped` on a constructor emits it into a second generated function, `<function>Scoped(registrar containercontract.ScopedRegistrar)`, through `MustRegisterScoped`/`MustRegisterScopedType`.


- security: `InMemoryTokenStore.WithRevocationEpochRetention` bounds how long `PurgeExpired` keeps a published revocation boundary after its instant, mirroring the rueidis store's option of the same name.
- security: every time-verifying component takes an injected clock — `JwtConfig.Clock`, `HmacTokenSourceConfig.Clock`, `HmacEnvelopeSignerConfig.Clock`, `TotpSecondFactorAuthenticatorConfig.Clock` and `NewMemoryNonceGuardWithClock` — defaulting to the system clock, the seam the token store and the rate limiter have always had.
- application: `ProcessContext` — `ServiceProcessContext`, `NewProcessContext`, `ProcessContextMustFromResolver`, `ProcessContextFromResolver` — is the console counterpart of the http request context


- http: `Kernel.HasErrorHandler` reports whether the application installed an error handler
- http: `Kernel.OpenRequestScopes` counts the request scopes opened and not yet closed — one per request being served, hijacked connections included — and the http shutdown drains on it within the shutdown budget.
- http: `RequestContextMustFromResolver` and `RequestContextFromResolver` resolve the current request's context — its id and start moment — from a request scope's resolver, the counterpart of `application.ProcessContextFromResolver` for the http process shape
- http: `RouteRegistry.SetBootCollisionRecorder` arms the aggregated boot-collision channel with `BootCollisionKindHttpRoute` and `BootCollisionKindHttpRouteName`
- bag: `ParameterBag.AppendString` adds one string under a name inside a single critical section, and `All` copies a stored string slice or string map instead of handing back the live value.



- application: an http process whose `MELODY_ENV` no `.env` artifact sets names it once when it starts serving, at `WARNING` under `bootWarning=application.environment.defaulted`, since the kernel reads the missing name as `dev` and serves with the debug tooling on.

### Changed

- documentation: `README.md`, the major's own readme, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `SECURITY.md` and the two GitHub templates say that `v3.14.0` is the last feature release of v3, that the next features ship in v4 and that v3 keeps receiving security fixes and patch-level defect fixes, where they said every new feature lands on v3 until v4 ships.
- security: **Behavioural change**: an access control rule path that does not begin with a slash is refused at declaration, by `accesscontrol.NewExactRule`, `NewSegmentPrefixRule`, `NewRawPrefixRule`, `NewRule`, the builder's `Require` and `AllowAnonymous` and the deprecated `security` constructors (`access control rule path must begin with a slash`): `Require("admin", "ROLE_ADMIN")` never matched the canonical request path, so its paths reached their handlers undecided. The empty raw prefix stays the fallback. A path with an empty, `.` or `..` segment is refused too (`access control rule path must be canonical`), the declared spelling checked before its trailing slash folds, so `/admin//` is refused by the path as written; `//admin` or `/x/../admin` governed nothing either.
- security: **Behavioural change**: a raw prefix rule declared with a trailing slash is refused at declaration, by `accesscontrol.NewRawPrefixRule("/api/", …)`, the builder's `Require` and the deprecated `security.NewAccessControlRule` (`access control raw prefix rule may not end with a slash`): the trimmed slash let the rule claim `/api-internal` and outrank a regex rule for that tree. The reach is declared instead, a segment prefix rule or the raw prefix without the slash; `/` stays the root.
- security: **Behavioural change**: `accesscontrol.NewControl` refuses a rule that carries no attribute, the zero `Rule`, which matched as the empty-prefix fallback and denied every path no other rule claimed.
- security: **Behavioural change**: `AuthenticatorTokenSource` dispatches `security.login.failure` when the authenticator that supported the request answers no user, a wrong `X-Api-Key` among them, and the request still goes on anonymous; the event carries `security credentials rejected`, and a failing listener fails the request. A pending second factor is not announced, so a lockout listener does not lock every enrolled user on the way to the prompt; a second factor supplied and refused is, carrying `security second factor rejected`.
- security: the firewall rules of a compiled firewall are checked through one `Firewall` built with it and the registry walks its firewalls in place, where each request copied the rule and firewall lists.
- security: **Behavioural change**: a global access control declared without any firewall now enforces instead of being silently dropped.
- security: **Behavioural change**: a zero-value `FirewallOverrideConfiguration{}` inherits the global access control as `NewFirewallOverrideConfiguration()` does, and gains the `WithAccessControl`, `WithRoleHierarchy`, `WithAccessDecisionManager`, `WithMergeStrategy` and `WithInheritGlobalAccessControl` setters, which read the constructor defaults when called on the zero value.
- security: **Behavioural change**: the role voters refuse a token that reports roles while answering `IsAuthenticated()` false — the shape a half-logged-in token takes.
- security: **Behavioural change**: `AccessDecisionManager.DecideAll` over an empty attribute list refuses instead of granting.
- security: **Behavioural change**: an access control rule whose attribute list normalizes to empty is refused at construction instead of being accepted.
- security: `totp.OtpauthURI` is now `totp.OtpauthUri`, the old spelling kept as a deprecated alias.
- security: **Behavioural change**: `PutWithTtl` on the in-memory token store refuses a non-positive ttl instead of storing the token forever.
- storage: **Behavioural change**: `LocalStorage` refuses, at every door, a key whose leaf is spelled exactly like one of its temp objects, `.melody-storage-<sha256>.<16 hex>.tmp`. Stored, such a key would be swept by a later `Put` of the key whose digest it carries.
- validation: **Behavioural change**: exceeding the nesting-depth cap is reported as a validation error (`nestingDepthExceeded`) only when the truncated subtree could carry a `validate` tag, so nesting past the cap no longer bypasses validation while tag-free client json is accepted up to the cap as before.
- logging: `RunShieldedStep`, `RunShieldedStepWithin` and `LogOnRecoverAndExitAfter` hand the step a `context.Context` carrying the deadline the shield holds it to, and the exit handler takes that budget as an argument, the budget spent in two halves: the step's deadline and the headroom in which a step that honoured it says what it could not release, where a step returning exactly on its budget was reported abandoned; the json logger takes its timestamp inside the write mutex with the encoding, so the stamps' order is the writes', and allocates less per record (`BenchmarkJsonLogger_Log`), the bytes of every record unchanged.
- config: **Behavioural change**: the comment cut matches godotenv's own, and a value that is EMPTY before a trailing comment reads as empty: `APP_SECRET= # fill this in`, the placeholder line of a copied `.env.example`, loaded `# fill this in` as the secret and a `$WORD` inside it failed the boot as an undefined reference; `KEY=#glued` stays data and a non-empty value keeps godotenv's countback.
- documentation: the example application inside this major keeps no changelog: `.example/README.md` is the whole of its documentation, a database left in an older shape is answered by `example:db:reset`, `CONTRIBUTING.md` says a change to an example is filed nowhere, and the citation band's corpus follows the document that went
- documentation: the version table names the date each line is supported until and the rule those dates come from, where v3's row read "v5 is released": the current line receives features, security fixes and deprecations, and the line a new major replaces receives security and patch-level defect fixes for eighteen more months from the successor's release. v3 carries no end date, its clock starting the day v4 ships; v1 is supported through 2027-08-17, v2 through 2027-09-08
- cli: **Behavioural change**: `--order=desc` reverses the listing in `debug:router`, `debug:events`, `debug:parameters`, `debug:middleware` and `debug:container`, applied before `--limit`/`--offset`, so a descending window returns the end of the list
- openapi: the schema mirror advertises as an unsatisfiable field what the validator now refuses, where it published a satisfiable bound the server never enforces: an empty `regex` pattern (`regex=`), which compiled `""` into a `pattern` matching every string; a numeric bound not an integer in its entirety (`parseBoundStrict`, the twin of the validator's `parseIntStrict`), so `max=99.5` read as `99` and `greaterThan=5x`; a negative `min`/`max`, null included, clamped to `minLength: 0` or keeping its null; the string constraints (`min`, `max`, `regex`, `email`, `alpha`, `numeric`, `alphanumeric`, `notBlank`) on a non-string shape; a tag that parses to no rule (`validate:","`).
- serializer: **Behavioural change**: a range carrying `q=0` refuses the types it covers rather than being ignored, and `ErrNotAcceptable` is answered only when every registered type is refused.
- serializer: **Behavioural change**: the accept header is read on the strict grammar the http readers share, `internal.ParseQualityValue` for `q` and `internal.SplitOutsideQuotes` for members and parameters, so `q=1e-1` and `q=1.5` lose their member
- serializer: **Behavioural change**: `NewSerializerManager` refuses a map in which two spellings collapse onto one normalized key, naming the normalized key and both spellings in sorted order.
- bag, http: **Behavioural change**: a request parameter that appeared once is stored as the string it is, a repeated one stays a string slice, and reading the repeated one as a single string answers its first value, as `Request.Input` and `url.Values.Get` do, where an earlier form of the branch panicked into a 500 through `StringOrDefault`/`HasNonEmptyString`; the whole list is read with `StringSlice`/`StringAt`
- session: the framework-wired session manager reads the KERNEL's clock, as the framework-wired session storage does, so a fixed-clock deployment moves the tombstone record and the entry expiry together; a manager the application wires itself defaults to the system clock, the reading `time.Since` used, in every constructor but the new clock-taking one.
- translation: **Behavioural change**: a plain placeholder whose parameter is ABSENT renders as the visible placeholder itself instead of as an empty string, and so do a plural and a select argument whose parameter is absent, where `{count, plural, …}` rendered its other branch with the number deleted (` items`) and `{gender, select, …}` its other branch; a parameter present with a nil value renders as before.
- container: `FromResolver` and `FromResolverByType` wrap a foreign error, from a resolver implemented outside the package or a provider's raw driver error, under the title `service resolution failed in resolver` with the error as the cause, where the title read `service not registered in resolver` for a registered service whose provider failed; a melody error travels out whole, and the service name or type is kept in the context.
- container: **Behavioural change**: the teardown breaks a tie the dependency graph leaves open on **creation order, latest first**, not on the node key descending.
- container: closing has two states, and a service's own `Close` may still resolve; `Has` and `HasType` answer under the suspension `Get` enforces; `Scope.RegisterScoped` refuses a closed container, as the other two registration doors do; a scope override answers the type-keyed resolutions of every type its name is registered under, as the container-level override does.
- container: **Behavioural change**: a closed container refuses new registrations and new overrides the way its scoped registrar always has.
- container: **Behavioural change**: an override must fit every type its name is registered under.
- container: **Behavioural change**: a typed-nil provider function is refused where it is registered, at the one gate all three registration paths go through.
- container: **Behavioural change**: two DIFFERENT types that share one identity key refuse to coexist, at the container door and the scoped one alike.
- container: **Behavioural change**: the protected `service.` namespace holds at both lifetimes.
- container: **Behavioural change**: the override assignability guard judges the value the way the readers will, on the scope as well as on the container.
- container: **Behavioural change**: a lazy handle follows the scope of the resolver it was built over instead of memoizing forever.

- cli: `cli.Root`, built by `cli.NewRoot` over `cli.NewCommandContext`, is a command tree that owns its command list: `Register` appends a command as `cli.Register` does on a root command, `SetWriter` and `SetErrorWriter` reach the tree and every command registered on it, whenever it was registered, and `Run` and `CommandNames` dispatch and list. The inert exit handler of `NewCommandContext` is the tree's, with no door to remove it.

- config, bag: **Behavioural change**: the string grammar of the float accessors is plain decimal — an optional sign, digits and at most one decimal point.
- bag: **Behavioural change**: `ParameterBag.Get` copies at the depth `All` copies — a stored `[]string` or `map[string]string` is answered as the caller's own.
- cache: `NewDefaultRememberOption` keeps the released detached flight; under the opt-in `WithCancelable(true)` the last waiter leaving cancels the flight and the next caller leads a fresh one, so a hung callback no longer owns its key for the process's life, but a wait timeout shorter than the callback never stores: pair a bounded wait on a slow callback with the default, or give the callback its own deadline. **C→v4**: the cancelable flight becomes the default
- cache: **Behavioural change**: `NewManager` builds a manager that does NOT own its backend, so `Close` leaves the backend open; `NewManagerOwningBackend` keeps the cascade.
- cache: **Behavioural change**: the key grammar is stated on the `cachecontract.Backend` contract — non-empty, no spaces, no newlines, at most 1024 bytes — and the in-memory backend enforces it with the refusals the redis backend always answered.
- cache: **Behavioural change**: the in-memory backend refuses four degenerate inputs it used to absorb.
- cache: **Behavioural change**: an existing counter payload is parsed against the redis integer grammar rather than Go's lenient one — an optional minus, a first digit of 1-9 unless the whole number is the single digit `0`, and nothing else.
- cache: **Behavioural change**: `Remember` coalesces concurrent callers only for pointer-kind `Cache` implementations, whose address tells two instances apart.
- cache: **Behavioural change**: the zero-value `&RememberOption{}` reads as `NewDefaultRememberOption()`.
- cache: **Behavioural change**: a `waitTimeout` of zero means no WAITING rather than no answer: a result the in-flight call has already memoized is taken without blocking, and only a flight still in the air answers with the timeout.
- cache: **Behavioural change**: `Remember` answers one shape on the miss and on every hit, the computing call returning the value passed through the manager's serializer round-trip.
- validation: **Behavioural change**: `regex`, `email`, `alpha`, `alphanumeric` and `numeric` refuse a value that is not a string instead of passing it in silence, which is the symmetry the numeric constraints already had.
- validation: **Behavioural change**: `minLength`, `maxLength` and `notBlank` measure a string rather than a Go rendering, and refuse a value of any other type.
- validation: **Behavioural change**: a rule that names a parameterized constraint WITHOUT parameters fails closed.
- validation: **Behavioural change**: `regex=` with an empty pattern is refused at construction.
- validation: **Behavioural change**: a negative bound is refused where it is written — at `NewMinLength`/`NewMaxLength` by panic, and at the tag door by a parse refusal, because a tag is data a request path reads.
- validation: **Behavioural change**: a numeric constraint parameter must be an integer in its entirety.
- validation: **Behavioural change**: a tag that survives the empty/skip-marker guard and then parses to no rule at all — a bare comma, for instance — is a syntax error rather than a request to validate nothing.
- openapi: **Behavioural change**: the schema mirror follows the repaired validator, so the generated document changes for every tag class the validation repairs above touch — in the restrictive direction, never the reverse.
- logging: **Behavioural change**: the json logger weighs a record whose level is not one of the five known ones as an error instead of inheriting the default lowest priority.
- logging: **Behavioural change**: the json logger's timestamp carries nanosecond precision.
- messagebus: the failure a transport whose `Close` panicked is reported under carries the stack of the panic, `panicStack`, as the container's close containment keeps it.
- messagebus: **Breaking**: the `Transport` contract's `Close` takes no runtime and answers only the error — `Close() error` — and a userland transport deletes the parameter: the container's teardown recognizes `Close() error` alone, so the old `Close(runtime) error` was never called and every deploy tore its broker connection down abruptly.
- messagebus: **Behavioural change**: `InMemoryTransport.Close` closes the delivery channel `Receive` handed out, so a consumer ranging over it sees the end of stream, where the channel stayed open forever and a consumer without its own cancellation blocked on `Close`; no send is ever selected onto the closed channel.
- messagebus: **Behavioural change**: `RouteType` refuses a nil or typed-nil transport at registration, in the framed form `RegisterTransports` uses for a nil map entry, where the first message of its type panicked on the dispatch path.
- messagebus: **Behavioural change**: a consumed message whose type has no registered handler fails the dispatch instead of being Acked with a warning, so a forgotten registration, or the pointer-vs-value keying trap, no longer drains a queue past the retry and dead-letter machinery; `HandleOptions.AllowMissingHandler` opts into the pass-through and `RequireHandler` stays, deprecated, reading as the default. **C→v4**: `RequireHandler` is removed.

- debug: `NewMiddlewareCommand` keeps its released one-provider form over `MiddlewareProvider`, and the description-and-build form is `NewMiddlewareCommandWithProviders`. **C→v4**: the two-provider form takes the released name and `MiddlewareProvider` is removed.
- event: **Behavioural change**: `AddSubscriber` refuses a second installation of one subscriber pointer through itself with "event subscriber is already registered", as v2.13.0 does, since `RemoveSubscriber` would take both zero-size twins down; `AddSubscriberWithRegistration` installs a subscriber twice, or a value subscriber. **C→v4**: the contract's pair takes and answers the registration.
- event: `NewEventDispatcherAdapter` keeps its released two-parameter signature, its clock refused when nil, typed nil included, and otherwise unused. **C→v4**: the constructor drops the clock parameter.
- event: **Behavioural change**: a dispatch that ended early with a listener marked required behind it returns `RequiredListenerSkippedError` instead of completing, or instead of returning only the ending listener's own failure.
- event: the propagation test opens the listener iteration instead of closing it.
- event: **Behavioural change**: marking a listener through `MarkListenerRequired` or `MarkListenerMaySkipRequiredListeners` panics on a registration the dispatcher does not hold, where it used to be a documented silent no-op
- event: **Behavioural change**: `AddSubscriber` validates every subscribed event before it registers a single listener, and refuses a subscriber that declares no events as well as an event name mapped to an empty list.
- debug: **Behavioural change**: under `--verbose` the json document of `debug:events` carries `data.{events, listeners}`, where it carried the event list alone.

- config: **Behavioural change**: a bare integer is refused where a duration is expected.
- config: **Behavioural change**: `Float64` refuses NaN and the infinities on every branch.
- config: a typed-nil `map[string]string` reads as the absence it is.
- config: **Behavioural change**: `IntWithDefault` answers the default only for a parameter that is ABSENT.
- config: a `.env` line is preprocessed byte by byte rather than through runes.
- config: **Behavioural change**: a `${...}` reference in a `.env` value whose closing brace arrived over a name outside the key grammar is refused instead of surviving as literal text.
- config: **Behavioural change**: an unterminated `%env(` and a `%name%` reference that nothing closed are both refused at boot.
- config: **Behavioural change**: `RegisterRuntime` refuses a blank name and one carrying leading or trailing whitespace.
- http: **Behavioural change**: `NewRequirements` refuses what it dropped, an empty parameter name, an empty pattern and a parameter declared twice, each named in the panic, keeping its value-variadic signature. **C→v4**: the signature takes the pointers the `Require*` helpers return.
- http: `JsonHandlerFailureResponder`, set through `WithJsonHandlerFailureResponder`, is handed the failure beside the status and the message; `JsonHandlerErrorResponder` keeps its four-parameter form. **C→v4**: the cause-taking form takes the name `JsonHandlerErrorResponder`.
- http: **Behavioural change**: a request path that names one parameter twice — `/orgs/:id/members/:id` — or carries a nameless `:` is refused at registration.
- http: **Behavioural change**: a route zone that is not one of the `RouteZone*` values is refused, at `ExposedRouteAttributes` and at registration, as are an exposed route with no name and an expose attribute that is not a bool.
- http: **Behavioural change**: a trusted-proxy entry that parses as neither a CIDR prefix nor an address is refused where the list is ingested, `Kernel.SetForwardedHeadersPolicy` and `NewForwardedClientIpResolver` alike.
- http: **Behavioural change**: route introspection publishes the requirement the caller DECLARED rather than the compiled form.
- http: **Behavioural change**: `JsonHandler` reads the request body through the same door `Request.BindJson` is.
- http: **Behavioural change**: every error the framework renders for a request — the exception listener's and the kernel's own `404` and `405` — goes through one door, negotiated against Accept, its json body carrying the request id beside the released `error` and `time`; `JsonErrorResponse`, which the security entry points answer through, keeps the `{error, time}` body unnegotiated, the request id on `X-Request-Id`. **C→v4**: the body becomes the structured envelope, `status` beside an `error` object carrying `message`, `context` and `cause`.
- http: **Behavioural change**: the public half of a validation failure's context travels under the key `validationErrors`
- http: **Behavioural change**: `JsonHandler` rejects a literal `null` request body with `400` when its request type is a pointer, a map, a slice, an interface, a func or a channel, the kinds a `null` decodes to nil.
- http: **Behavioural change**: a route pattern whose optional parameter is not its final segment is refused at registration instead of being accepted and half-served.
- http: the response path saves and advertises the session published under `RequestAttributeSession` when the response is written, not the one captured before routing; a header key the response names is owned by the response; `Router.Match` hands out a deep copy of the winning route's attributes instead of the registry's own map; the response the terminate event and the access log report is the one the client received.
- http: `TokenBucketLimiter` is now `FixedWindowLimiter`, the old type name and both constructors kept as deprecated aliases.
- http: **Behavioural change**: the request attributes the kernel owns are published after the route attributes, so a route attribute no longer replaces the session object or the resolved scheme, which is `_scheme`; `RequestAttributeSession` keeps its released value `session`. **C→v4**: the session attribute becomes `_session`.
- http: **Behavioural change**: a urlencoded body whose read or parse failed is refused before the handler, 413 when the size limit stopped the read and 400 for a body the client broke, as the json binding refuses it.
- http: **Behavioural change**: a session-storage outage on the save path answers 500 rather than the handler's response, the session cookie suppressed either way.
- http: **Behavioural change**: two routes identical on everything the matcher discriminates — pattern, methods, host, schemes, locales, requirements and priority — are refused at registration, the later one never dispatchable.
- http: **Behavioural change**: a handler-returned failure files one record at the level it deserves — a deliberate 4xx at warning, a client's own cancellation named as such at warning, everything else at error — and marks the error, so the exception listener attaches its request coordinates instead of filing the failure again.
- http: a response status outside net/http's `[100, 999]` is refused by name on both write paths, the public `WriteToHttpResponseWriter` answering the error its signature promises before anything is written, the internal path logging and answering the rendered 500, where `WriteHeader` panicked; `AllowedMethods` applies the matcher's locale filter through the shared `matchesLocale`, so the methods announced for a path no longer name locale-scoped routes it cannot reach.
- http: **Behavioural change**: the kernel's configuration doors and the router's registration doors are BOOT-ONLY and refuse rather than corrupt.
- http: **Behavioural change**: a route is matched on the path as the client SPELLED it — `URL.RawPath` while it still unescapes to `URL.Path`, else `URL.EscapedPath` — each segment unescaped after the split.
- http: **Behavioural change**: `matchesHost` compares host names case-insensitively, the port significant only when the route declared one.
- http: **Behavioural change**: route defaults are merged BEFORE the locale gate reads them, in `Match` and in `AllowedMethods` alike.




- logging: **Behavioural change**: `LogOnRecover` only logs.
- config: **Behavioural change**: `Resolve()` reports an error once the application is serving.
- config: **Behavioural change**: a positive session ttl below one second fails the boot.
- httpclient: **Behavioural change**: the client sets `MaxIdleConnsPerHost` on its transport, exposed as `TransportConfig.MaxIdleConnsPerHost` and defaulting to `MaxIdleConns` (100), following a positive override of it unless set itself, where net/http fell back to two per host; a caller relying on the two-connection ceiling sets `MaxIdleConnsPerHost` explicitly.
- httpclient: **Behavioural change**: the response body cap bounds `RequestStream` too, the inherited default of ten mebibytes included
- httpclient: **Behavioural change**: `buildUrl` resolves the target against the base url by RFC 3986 reference resolution, the rule Symfony and Guzzle implement, instead of joining the base as a prefix: a base url whose path lacks its trailing slash panics at construction, and an absolute-path target under a base with a path names a different resource than the prefix join did.
- httpclient: `TransportConfig` keeps its released value fields, a non-positive one taking the default, `MaxIdleConnsPerHost` among them. **C→v4**: the fields become pointers built with `TransportDuration`/`TransportCount`, so a set zero reaches net/http as zero.
- httpclient: **Behavioural change**: header maps are canonicalized at the door and two spellings that collapse onto one header are refused by name, as the serializer refuses its mime keys: the constructor panics at the wiring, and `SetHeaders`, a request-time door, writes nothing from a colliding map and the request fails with `request option refused` naming the option's index, where a panic bypassed the caller's handling.
- httpclient: **Behavioural change**: `RequestOptions.Headers` and `Query` hand out copies, and the contract says so: the setters are the one door that writes.

- validation: **Behavioural change**: a nil pointer embed is validated as "nothing was supplied" rather than "nothing to validate", so the constraints on its promoted fields run against their zero values exactly as a value embed's already did.


- cli: **Behavioural change**: in json mode a command's standard output is the rendered document and nothing else — the ansi start/finish banner that `cli.Register` wraps around every registered command is suppressed, and `--format=json` implies `--no-color`.
- cli: **Behavioural change**: a command whose envelope reports an error exits non-zero.
- cli: **Behavioural change**: `--format` and `--order` reject an unrecognised value instead of silently substituting the default.



- rate limit: **Behavioural change**: the default key extractor now keys on the client IP alone instead of `ip:path`. Both limiters also bound how many distinct keys they track (`SetMaxKeys`, default 1,000,000): once the map is full and an idle-entry prune frees nothing, a request under an unseen key is denied rather than growing the map without bound.

- cli: **Behavioural change**: `--format=json` writes one document on one line, terminated by a newline, instead of an indented block.
- cli: **Behavioural change**: text of server or client origin reaching the terminal is escaped rather than obeyed.

- mailer: **Behavioural change**: configured smtp credentials fail closed when the server does not advertise `AUTH`, whatever `RequireAuth` says.


- debug: **Behavioural change**: `debug:container` and `debug:middleware` describe by default and build only under `--build`.
- debug: **Behavioural change**: the `--build` sweep reports its failures on the envelope, so `app debug:container --build --format=json || exit 1` fails a deployment gate the way the single-service door always has
- debug: `debug:version` reads the version rows off the envelope meta, where `NewMeta` has already applied the fallback chain — the command's explicit value, then the process-wide declaration made through `output.SetApplicationVersion`, then nothing.

- serializer: **Behavioural change**: a manager deliberately configured without `application/json` answers an empty or unmatched accept header with its first configured serializer in lexical mime order, where it refused with `no default serializer configured` and the result handler served hardcoded json, the representation the configuration had excluded. See UPGRADE.md.
- example: **Behavioural change**: the object storage reads `S3_INSECURE` (`true` dials plain http) in place of `S3_SECURE`; a `.env` that carried `S3_SECURE` is read as secure until the key is renamed.

### Deprecated

- cli: `--fields` and `--sort`, `output.Option.Fields` and `SortKey`, `output.Flags.Fields` and `SortKey`, `output.FlagNameFields`, `output.FlagNameSortKey` and `output.SplitFields` are deprecated: the two flags are declared and parsed as at v3.13.0, and no printer reads them; they are withdrawn in v4.
- security: the four access control rule constructors are superseded by `security/accesscontrol`, whose constructors name their reach and take a config struct: `NewAccessControlRule` by `accesscontrol.NewRawPrefixRule`, `NewAccessControlRuleWithSegmentPrefix` by `NewSegmentPrefixRule`, `NewAccessControlExactRule` by `NewExactRule`, `NewAccessControlRegexRule` by `NewRegexRule`. All four keep working and forward to their replacement, so they refuse what it refuses (a trailing slash on a raw prefix, an unanchored `PUBLIC_ACCESS` regex); they are removed in v4, and moving to the replacement on v3 carries no change across that cut

### Removed

- application: **Breaking**: `HttpTimeoutConfiguration` and `HttpShutdownConfiguration` are removed, and only code naming either type stops compiling: no application could supply one, since the configuration `NewApplication` builds implemented neither; the shutdown timeout is read from `MELODY_HTTP_SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT`.
- cli: the dead unexported error presenter is gone, an error-printing path nothing called since the envelope renderer became the one presentation
- session: burying a tombstone prunes only the lapsed burials, in the order they happened, instead of sweeping the whole record on every call; the in-memory storage sweeps expired sessions in chunks, releasing the lock between them and reading each entry again under the chunk's lock
- config: the unexported `loadRequiredDotEnvFile` is gone, a required-file loader nothing called on any major.

- session: **Behavioural change**: `NewManager` does not close the storage it was handed.
- session: **Behavioural change**: `Manager.SaveSession` and `Manager.DeleteSession` hold a lock keyed to the session id across the storage call rather than one lock for the whole manager.
- http: a session another request ended while this one was running is recorded at **warning**, under its own name, and the response path expires the browser cookie and serves the handler's response unchanged.

### Fixed

- documentation: `SECURITY.md` states that a path no access control rule claims reaches its handler undecided and names the fallback refusing it (the `Control.Match` GoDoc said the caller decides), that an impersonation request lacking the header, the authentication or the switch role keeps the caller's token while a resolver error or unusable target answers an anonymous one, and, with the GoDoc of `NewJwtTokenValidatorWithRevocationEpoch`, that it sets `RejectFutureIssuedAt` whatever the config says.
- documentation: CONTAINER.md lists the scopes as their own entry, names `RegisterScoped` as the one every scope implements through `ScopedRegistrar`, not the `Scope` contract, and states the teardown walk's bounds; HTTP.md names `security.KernelFirewallListenerPriority` and `security.KernelAccessControlListenerPriority` beside the cors listener's priorities; the `runtime` package document says what it carries, the GoDoc of `DefaultTeardownTimeout` and `RunShieldedStep` that a step gets half a positive budget as its deadline, and that of `RunExclusive` that the callback is cancelled at the lease's demotion instant; `LOCK.md` says what a lost Redis reply whose give-back fails costs; `DEBUG.md` no longer says the action closes `runtimeInstance.Container()`, and its standalone examples and those of `CLI.md` close the container, since `cli.Register` closes only the scope.
- documentation: HTTP.md rejoins the `RateLimitMiddleware` bullet split inside its link to `SimpleRateLimitWithResolver`, `IpRateLimitWithResolver` and `UserRateLimitWithResolver`, and SESSION.md the tombstone bullet a `RegenerateSession` bullet split; SERIALIZER.md says an `Accept` header of malformed members gets the default representation, only one refusing every type erroring with `ErrNotAcceptable`; `HTTP.md` and `BAG.md` say `bag.String` answers a repeated key's first value and `("", false)` for an unconvertible one, not a panic and `("", true)`; `CONFIG.md` says an empty `MELODY_ENV` selects the development files then fails the boot, not that it defaults to `dev`; the package documents state the conditions they left out (the CORS preflight test, a rotation-refused save keeping the cookie, `SetBackplane` re-accepting an identified backplane, the zero teardown budget on a recovered panic, `WithCollectionPriority`, `LeaderGateOptions` with `MaxConsecutiveRefreshFailures`, the consumer's `--concurrency`, `NewReopenableFileWriter`'s error, the openapi warning only with `--out`, the module hook order, the example's `handler/event`).
- documentation: MESSAGEBUS.md lists the six fields of `RetryPolicy` and says the backoff is capped at `MaxDelay` and a rejected message requeued after the next step, or after `FailureRequeueDelay` when `BaseDelay` is zero; LOGGING.md says the exit hook runs under its caller's budget, 10 seconds only when none is declared.
- documentation: the block's markers follow one rule — behavioural for what an application running `v3.13.0` observes, breaking for a compile break, neither for a repair of what the released contract already called wrong — and the 21 `**Behavioural change**` markers the 08-28 compression deleted from the `[Unreleased]` entries where someone must act are restored: the boot refusals of a route or module from a boot hook, of `RegisterConfiguration` under any name but `logging`, of a scoped `service.` registration and of a reused `RouteOptions`; the wiring generator on a dead `--strict` exclude and a mistyped `//melody:` directive; `debug:container --build` failing a gate, `--limit` and `--offset`; a shared `ParameterizedConstraint`, a concurrent session `Storage`, a lazy handle over a closed scope, `MaxIdleConnsPerHost` following `MaxIdleConns`, a duplicated flag name, a mismatched table row; the json logger's unknown level, the handler failure record, nesting past the depth cap, an opaque token without an issue instant, the OpenAPI document, an absent translation parameter. `NewAccessControlRule` refusing `PUBLIC_ACCESS` and `LogOnRecoverAndExitAfter` gain entries, `buildUrl` is behavioural not breaking, `MELODY_HTTP_SESSION_TTL` unmarked, and the rueidis, bunorm and amqp duplicates removed. `UPGRADE.md` gains a section for every marked core and integration change that had none (among them `Unlimited` on the mysql and pgsql zero fields), names the manifest's `--out` anchoring and the undefined `${X}` refusal, and drops two sections no released integration had.
- documentation: the README's minimal sample writes a one-line `.env`, and it and `VERSION.md` build one main package, since `go build -o app ./...` refuses several; the README states the frozen majors' back-port rule, any patch-sized defect fix plus security fixes until v4; `UPGRADE.md` covers `MELODY_TEARDOWN_TIMEOUT`, the latching `Clear`, `JsonHandler`'s `null` refusal, the padded path, a lazy handle's `Closed` or `IsClosed`, `SetBackplane`, a `q` past three decimals (as `SERIALIZER.md` says), the registry teardown budget and two quoted refusals; the example's README names its scheduled commands, `product:list`'s arguments, its directories, gates, containers and ports; `APPLICATION.md`'s module example takes `containercontract.Resolver` where `func(serviceLocator any)` was refused at boot, and says `RegisterConfiguration` takes one name; `WIRING.md` uses `RegisterCliCommand`, not an undeclared `RegisterCommand`; `ROADMAP.md` drops three shipped items; `DOCUMENTATION.md` carries five missing passages (among them `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/` and the `Exported API` exception) and names its `README.md` the **module** root, which `../README.md` resolves to.
- documentation: the GoDoc of `ServerSentEventHub`, `session.Manager`, `session.InMemoryStorage`, `validation.Validator`, `event.EventDispatcher`, `config.Configuration`, `http.Router` and `http.RouteRegistry` says what the type holds and what protects it, as `CONTAINER.md` asks; fifty-six always-carried exported doors are listed, as on the published majors, and fifteen symbols documented: `cache.Item`, `cache.NewItem`, `logging.LoggerFromResolver`, `LoggerMustFromResolver`, `output.NewTablePrinter`, `NewMeta`, `NewWarning`, `NewError`, `NewErrorCause`, `security.NewFirewall`, `NewFirewallManager`, `NewFirewallRegistry`, `application.MiddlewareFactory`, `RouteRegistrar`, `SecurityModule`; `CACHE.md`, `SERIALIZER.md` and `CLOCK.md` state what this major does; the test comments cite no `(§5.N)` of the unpublished lesson register.
- cli: **Behavioural change**: a flag spelling declared twice, an empty alias, a spelling of the help flag (`help`, `h`) and a nil flag are refused by name at registration, through `cli.Register` and `Root.Register`, and at every `cli.DispatchCommand`, with `cli flag spelling declared twice`, `cli flag alias is empty` or `cli flag may not be nil`, where the first declaration won in silence and a flag spelled `h` ran the command for `-h`.
- cli: **Behavioural change**: `--verbosity`, `--limit`, `--offset` and `--table-width` parse in base ten, where `--limit=010` read as eight, `--limit=08` was refused and `--limit=0x10` read as sixteen; a base prefix is refused as syntax, and a command's own integer flag declares its base.
- cli: **Behavioural change**: `cli.NewCommandContext` installs an inert exit handler, so a returned error, an exit-coded one included, reaches the application's exit path rather than `os.Exit` inside the engine's `Run`; a caller wanting the engine's default sets `ExitErrHandler` back.
- cli: **Behavioural change**: `output.MergeFlags` refuses a duplicated flag name at the line that declares it, and refuses a nil flag.
- cli: **Behavioural change**: `TableBlockBuilder.AddRow` refuses a row whose cell count disagrees with the block's declared columns, naming the block and both counts.
- cli: **Behavioural change**: the table format prints the warnings under `--quiet` too, and renders the envelope error whole — message, code, details and cause.
- cli: **Behavioural change**: the `[error]` line of a failing command goes to the command's `ErrWriter`, standard error by default, in every format, where it ended a redirected document and `--format=json` wrote none.
- cli: printing: a json document or table the sink took only in part fails the command with `io.ErrShortWrite` through `output.Render`, as `io.Copy` reads it, where a truncated report exited zero; the table printer sizes cells in display cells through `internal.DisplayWidth`, a multi-line cell by its widest line; the banner honours `quiet`, and the banners and the `command not found` header honour `--no-color`; the header escapes the command name, and `[failed]` is coloured after the escaping, where it printed `\x1b[31m` literally; a panicking command prints the failed banner, not `[finished] [success]`.
- cli: the exit path: an exit-coded error exits through the application's shutdown and logging instead of `os.Exit` in the cli library, the container left to the exit handler; a failure whose container or scope close also fails keeps the exit code and its close failures; an envelope-reported failure reaches the log, and an unprintable result over a reported failure is journaled with both; an error behind a typed nil pointer reads as success. debug: `debug:middleware` re-raises an exit error a factory panics with. event: a listener added through the inspecting adapter is named by its own function.
- cli: the standard integer flags `--verbosity`, `--limit`, `--offset` and `--table-width` refuse a negative value naming the flag, as `--format` and `--order` refuse theirs, and `output.NormalizeOption` clamps a negative `VerbosityLevel`; `output.NewMeta` keeps a supplied `version.Go`; `DispatchCommand` reads `-vv` as level 2, and a positional `help` or `h` reaches the command, `--help` still printing usage.
- example: **Behavioural change**: without `AMQP_DSN` `/messagebus/dispatch` and `/outbox/relay` answer 503 naming the key and `melody:outbox:relay` refuses naming it, where they published into a process-local queue nothing read; the outbox keeps its rows until a broker is configured, and `messagebus:dispatch` keeps its in-process round trip.
- example: **Behavioural change**: the history door answers, beside the archive, the current reading the scheduled refresh cached, or one taken on a cold cache; a reading is cached for the refresh interval, at least the schedule's hour and five minutes, where it lapsed unread after five minutes.
- example: **Behavioural change**: the catalogue database is opened at its first use rather than at boot, as the archive is, so a process that never reads it, one serving only `/health` included, opens no connection; a declared catalogue that cannot be opened is refused, named with its location, by the first request or command reading it, where the boot refused it.
- example: **Behavioural change**: the example's one schema migration is amended, so a volume built by an earlier checkout is refused by its fingerprint until `example:db:reset --force` rebuilds it; it holds the currency code unique, a product's currency and category by foreign keys `ON DELETE RESTRICT`, the username folded in Go in a `username_normalized` column with its unique key (in place of `LOWER(username)`), an identifier sequence and a per-account session index.
- example: **Behavioural change**: a currency's code is its identity: a second currency under a taken code or a rename onto one, and the delete of a currency a product is priced in, are refused with 409, and a product write naming a missing category or currency is a 400, where they stored a wrong `?currency=` conversion or answered 500; `CategoryService.DeleteById` refuses a category a product sits in with `ErrCategoryInUse`; the check and the write share the catalogue lock, and the write doors validate the trimmed spelling they store.
- example: sign-in and accounts: a session whose account lookup failed is served anonymous with the session kept, its cause journaled at Error; a second factor that cannot be read is answered 503 `the second factor is unavailable`, not 500, its outage journaled once, a token naming no loaded account answers 500 with its cause journaled, and a failed budget release reaches the journal through the limiter's new logger option; a recovery code is compared in canonical form, so one typed in upper case or without its hyphen is accepted; the sign-in and account write doors answer an oversized body 413 `payload too large`, not 400 `invalid json`; an unanswering directory is filed at Error as an infrastructure failure; the admin update and delete doors read the account under the row's lock, decide the refusal of a peer administrator on the account the write changes and write only the fields the body names, so a stale cache no longer restores a replaced password hash or a revoked role, and a found entity is cached for at most ten minutes.
- example: the catalogue writes: a minted identifier that lost to a supplied one is minted again, a supplied one at the ceiling refused 400, and a deleted entity's identifier is never reused, the mint continuing past the highest ever stored, and a failed sequence raise refuses the create with nothing written; a price is held below 10^12, NaN included, where a near-ceiling one answered 500 to the list; a write whose event fails after the commit answers the stored row, journals the failure at Error with the event and the entity and drops the changed cache entries, an account write still failing; a deleted product's view counter is dropped; two concurrent deletes serialise, the second answering 404 with no event, and a delete and its audit entry commit together; a read under a spelling the collation folds, `prod-1 `, answers 404 and caches nothing; a rename whose row cannot be read back still publishes, and the event stream answers 503 on a shut-down hub.
- example: proxies and budgets: a name on `APP_TRUSTED_PROXY_LIST` that did not resolve is retried five seconds later off the request path, where every client behind the balancer shared one budget for a minute, and the shutdown waits for the retry and starts no other; `/messagebus/dispatch`, `/outbox/enqueue`, `/outbox/relay`, `POST /storage/object` and `/events/publish` spend the per-address write budget; the hourly request budget tracks at most 10,000 addresses, an unseen one taking the oldest slot rather than being locked out.
- example: the object store and the output: a store that cannot be read is answered 500 with its cause journaled, 404 only for an absent object; a presigned ttl past the hour is refused 400 before it can wrap; a key carrying a `..` segment is answered 400 before the store is asked; an outbound url carrying an `@` anywhere, `feed:secret@host` included, is marked secret; `product:list` and the text/plain refusal escape control characters, so a name can neither forge a row nor repaint the terminal.
