- example: the databases: a request that changed nothing no longer resolves the catalogue journal, so a journal whose migration is refused fails only the requests that changed something, and a trail that cannot be resolved or flushed under a failed handler is journaled with the handler's own error answered; the audited seed is one transaction, and one that lost to another process reads the count again; the catalogue's and archive's migrations take a lock each; a schema set writes its row as building before its first statement and seals it after its last, so an interrupted run is finished by the next where only `example:db:reset --force` cleared it, a sealed volume whose bookkeeping was lost is recorded as migrated, and one missing a table or of another schema is still refused, the record tables gaining a `state` column a volume built before it is reset for; the catalogue opens under the archive's request-sized retry budget; `example:user:create` refuses without the catalogue database, where it printed `created user` into the console's own memory.
- http: **Behavioural change**: `SetCookie` panics on a cookie name that is not a valid token, as on an empty one, where the standard library wrote an empty `Set-Cookie`; a `SameSite=None` session cookie is written `Secure` under `SessionCookieSecureFromScheme` whatever the scheme, since browsers drop a `None` cookie without it, `SessionCookieSecureNever` still honoured; a session response whose `Cache-Control` leaves a quote open is answered `Cache-Control: no-store, private`; the compression middleware reads level 0, `NoCompression`, as the default and closes a stalled source when the client leaves; the static file server names a refused open, a fifo, a socket or a device by its cause; the GoDoc of `DeleteCookie`, `RateLimitConfig` and `NewOptions` says what each does.
- http: **Behavioural change**: a catch-all keeps an encoded separator inside the segment the client put it in, so `/files/a%2Fb/c` and `/files/a/b/c` no longer bind the same `a/b/c`; only the separator is re-escaped, and a literal `%2F` sent as `%252F` binds as an encoded separator.
- http: **Behavioural change**: `melody:routes:manifest` mirrors the openapi generate command in the four places that decide whether its artifact is trustworthy, none of which it did while its own comment said it mirrored that command.
- http: **Behavioural change**: a middleware may only reference, through `before` or `after`, a middleware enabled in at least every environment it is itself enabled in.
- http: **Behavioural change**: a `RouteGroup` no longer writes its name prefix and its merged requirements and defaults back into the caller's `RouteOptions`.
- http: **Behavioural change**: equal-priority middlewares now run in the order the application registered them.
- http: **Behavioural change**: `Kernel.SetSessionCookiePolicy` no longer drops the `SameSite=Lax` default when the caller names only `Path` or `Domain`.
- http: **Behavioural change**: a response that carries the session cookie is kept out of shared caches — `public` is dropped and `private` added unless it already says `private` or `no-store` — since a shared cache replaying it handed another client the first one's session id; the expiring cookie of a cleared or deleted session is kept out the same way, and the three doors that write the session cookie share one spelling of it.
- http: **Behavioural change**: a route pattern written in a parameter syntax the router does not speak is refused at registration.
- http: **Behavioural change**: a route whose locale declaration contradicts its pattern is refused at registration, in both directions.
- http: server-sent events: `ServerSentEventHub.SetBackplane(nil)` waits for the publishes inside the backplane, as `Shutdown` does, bounded by thirty seconds with a warning naming them, where a `Publish` that never returned held the clear and every backplane's `Close` for ever; `ServerSentEventHub.SetBackplane` refuses a second backplane by name whatever its type, where `==` panicked on a non-comparable one, and `SetBackplane` reads a typed nil as nothing; `ServerSentEventHub.Shutdown` closes the backplane it owns, a broadcast past the closed check finishes before it, a close with a spent budget no longer reports publishes in flight over a quiet hub, and one whose deadline runs out under a live `ServerSentEventBackplane.Publish` hands the backplane to a detached closer instead of dropping it; the zero `ServerSentEventHub` subscribes instead of panicking; the writer is safe for concurrent use, refuses a stream it cannot serve before it commits and the event shapes the grammar reads otherwise, is poisoned by a frame that failed partway, builds no replacer per frame and asks a writer that cannot take a write deadline only once, and `NewServerSentEventWriter` probes the flush capability through the wrapper chain.
- http: error rendering: every failed request files its own record, even over an error other requests return too — the kernel marks a carrier of the request's occurrence, which `IsAlreadyLogged`, `errors.Is` and `errors.As` read through, not the shared error — and `FromResolver` and `MustGet` keep the name of the service that failed; the error body is negotiated as the success path is, against every line of a repeated `Accept` (`Header.Get` read the first), `text/plain` reading lines where it read a Go map dump, the kernel's own `405` (keeping `Allow`) and `404` included, with the request id on json; an error the application's error handler declines keeps its own status, `exception.NotFound` or an `HttpException` no longer served as a 500, and an `Accept` refusing every representation never masks an error's status behind a 406, the status read once through the guarded door; an `OPTIONS` the method policy answers is journaled at debug, not as a refused method at warning, and a path the canonical guard refuses leaves its refusal record alone, without a no-route record beside it; the validation detail `BindJsonAndValidate` attached under `validationErrors` reaches the json and text/plain bodies through `WithoutRuleWiringContext`; a 4xx a handler returned is recorded at warning; `PrefersHtml` reads `Accept` under the serializer's rules and answers html only above json and text/plain, or at an equal weight the client wrote first, so `text/plain, text/html;q=0.5` no longer gets the page, an `Accept` or `Accept-Encoding` cut at the member cap reads as unparsable, and a `q` with trailing zeros past the third decimal keeps its member, `application/json;q=1.0000` reading as `q=1.0` while `q=0.0000` still refuses; the text/plain body is bounded in depth, lines, values and bytes, a self-holding map is written with an ellipsis, and a `String` or `Error` that panics is answered with the json envelope, the exception listener containing such a panic in the error text and the debug cause as its siblings do; a `JsonHandler` responder that panics leaves its refusal standing, and `JsonHandler` can no longer turn a refused request into a success; the request fields of every diagnostic record are cut at 512 bytes.
- http: the kernel's recording writer: an informational status other than `101`, such as `103 Early Hints`, no longer commits the header, so the response returned after it is written, where the client read an empty `200`; a `WriteHeader` after the commit no longer rewrites the recorded status; and a flush reaches the connection through every wrapper that implements `Unwrap` without a `Flush` of its own, where it was a silent no-op, a failed flush still recording the commit.
- http: the kernel's request lifecycle: the response a `kernel.response` listener swapped in is the one the recovery of `ServeHttp` closes, once, and a `FileResponse` or `ServeReader` built before a panic is closed even when a listener leaves by the re-raised exit error; a response discarded for an out-of-range status, which left an `*os.File` open, by an outer middleware panicking after `next()`, or by the abort sentinel is closed, and a discarded body whose `Close` panics is contained; a handler panicking with `net/http`'s `ErrAbortHandler` aborts the connection silently instead of a 500; the session is loaded inside the recovery guard, a storage outage answering a logged `500`, read through `IsNilInterface` and not stored for a discarded response; the application's error handler runs under the kernel's recovery, a last guard covers the window the main one cannot, and the record carries the panic site's stack; the kernel fails closed when a `kernel.request` or `kernel.controller` listener stopped propagation ahead of a required one; a handler returning no response reaches `kernel.response`; a scope-close failure without a request logger reaches the emergency logger; the pipeline build report travels with its refusal; a typed-nil `Response` or `*Response`, a typed-nil request at `PrefersHtml`, `RegenerateRequestSession`, `Service.IsPreflight`, `Service.RequestOrigin`, `FileServer.Serve`, `FileServer.ServeReader` and the listeners behind them, and a nil-pointer `Attributes()` bag whose `Get` was called are answered as nil instead of dereferenced; `router.Match` is read through its nil guard and `exception.IsAlreadyLogged` at every reader, and `RecoverToError` normalizes a typed-nil error while an `Error()` that panics degrades to a named rendering failure.
- http: routing: `FilterRouteManifestByZone` refuses a whitespace zone by name as given, and `melody:routes:manifest` hands `--zone` over untrimmed, where `--zone "  "` wrote the manifest whole; the manifest says its requirements are unanchored, so a consumer anchors them itself, `new RegExp(requirement).test(value)` matching a substring; `melody:routes:manifest` declares the standard flag set; a duplicate route an aggregating boot records no longer enters the tree under another route's index; a route bound to a bracketed IPv6 literal is reachable behind a port; a route whose only segment is a trailing optional parameter is served at the root again, and an empty path reads as the root; a path differing from a route only by surrounding whitespace no longer reaches it, an empty segment no longer satisfies a named parameter, so `/users//profile` misses `/users/:id/profile`, and the asterisk-form `OPTIONS` is no longer offered to every `/:param` route; an empty method adds no token to the `Allow` of a `405`; `UrlGenerator.GeneratePath` substitutes a non-empty default for a parameter supplied empty.
- http: the static file server tags a file without a modification time, as an embedded one, by its size and a digest of its bytes, where the build version answered 304 over a changed asset (`GenerateEtag` is unchanged); it answers `GET` and `HEAD` only, no longer trims whitespace off the path it resolves (`/%20internal/secret.json` served `internal/secret.json`), refuses a path that is not canonical instead of serving what `path.Clean` folds it onto, and records a refused resolution on the streaming path; `If-None-Match` matches its tag anywhere in the list and in the weak form, answering `304 Not Modified`, and wins over `If-Modified-Since`, which accepts all three HTTP date formats; `FileServerConfig.SetExcludedPathList` normalizes an entry without a leading slash.
- http: the compression middleware contains a panic of the body reader on its copy goroutine, where it took the process down; it emits `Vary: Accept-Encoding` on every path, a response already carrying `Content-Encoding` included, bounds the read that decides the threshold, pools its gzip writers in one `sync.Pool` per level and survives a nil header map; `acceptsGzip` reads Accept-Encoding under the one negotiation grammar.
- http: rate limiting and the client address: the sliding window limiter clamps an instant recorded out of order, so a wall clock moving backwards no longer refills a login budget, and `SlidingWindowLimiter.Allow` frees the expired prefix through `sort.Search`; the idle prune holds its threshold past the midpoint of the duration range; the middleware inspects what its limit handler answered; `RegisterRateLimitRequestListener` meters every request on kernel.request, before authentication; `NewForwardedClientIpResolver` reads a bracketed IPv6 literal without a port, where every IPv6 client fell onto the proxy's own budget, and it and `SetForwardedHeadersPolicy` copy the trusted-proxy list.
- http: requests, cookies and records: a urlencoded body past net/http's 10 MiB under a larger configured limit is parsed by the framework and published as `PostForm`, which `ParseForm` merges into `Form`, and a legacy semicolon in the query no longer makes `ParseForm` refuse a valid body with `400`; `BindJson` saturates its one-byte allowance at `MaxInt64` instead of wrapping, its refusals carrying their causes, and `BindJsonAndValidate` refuses a `null` body with the 400 `empty request body` `JsonHandler` answers; a `*MaxBytesError` from `ParseMultipartForm` is answered 413; a handler's own `Set-Cookie` survives beside the session cookie, `SetCookie` writes through a nil header map, and the session guard adds `private` to every `Cache-Control` line without rewriting them, honouring quotes, where `Get` and `Set` kept one line and `no-cache="X-One, Public, X-Two"` came back `no-cache="X-One, X-Two"`; the session-persistence records carry a truncated SHA-256 of the session id; the access log records the scheme the forwarded-headers policy resolved; a response-write failure or a handler cancellation the client caused is filed at warning, as `request cancelled by client` only under net/http's plain cancellation, one the application set with a cause staying at error.
- openapi: **Behavioural change**: a relative `--out` is anchored at the project directory, exactly as the wiring command has always anchored its own, and the parent directories are created on the way.
- openapi: the document describes the routes the router serves: of two patterns converging on one path, the one with the higher `Priority()` owns it, the earlier winning a tie, an optional tail's mirror competing alike; every prefix left by trailing optional segments is described, so `/a/:x?/:y?` is `/a`, `/a/{x}` and `/a/{x}/{y}`, with an operation id naming the omitted parameters (`a.show.without.x.y`), and `{name?}` inside braces no longer expands; an unnamed route has no operationId where two shared `.get`, and a named route with no method list gets all eight verbs, each with its own operationId; an unmodelled verb is named in the description, a catch-all path stops at its segment, a colliding path no longer replaces an earlier route's operations, and an unknown rule, a typo or one from `RegisterConstraint`, is named in the field's description, which says the server may refuse values the schema admits. A consumer diffing documents sees new paths, moved operations and dropped ids.
- openapi: the schema mirror follows the validator, held by an executable proof: a field rejecting every value is `required`; `notEmpty` behind a pointer to a fixed array drops `minItems` as the value form does, on a plain `[N]T` is no longer `required` with `minItems 1`, on a zero-length array is unsatisfiable, and on a component reference reads the component; a type reaching itself through a named collection (`type Metadata map[string]Metadata`, `type Nodes []Nodes`) is a reference, not a stack overflow, and a self-pointer no longer live-locks; a property promoted below a diamond is published; an embedded `time.Time` codec is the `date-time` string `encoding/json` writes; an instantiated generic gets a valid key and a resolving `$ref`; an unsigned integer gets a zero floor; a `validate` tag is parsed once per tag rather than per predicate on every `SpecHandler` request.
- openapi: the registry shares no memory: `Registry.Describe` stores and `Get` answers copies of `Tags` and `Responses`, each operation of a multi-method route gets its own tags and path parameters, so a write into one no longer rewrites another; a `Describe` once `Run` has begun is refused with "may not describe a route after the application started serving", where it raced the spec handler into a fatal concurrent map write, a hand-built unmarked registry still admitting it.
- openapi: `melody:openapi:generate` writes through the command writer, wraps a marshal failure, and without `--out` journals the missing-info-service warning through the application's logger, the emergency one on stderr when the journal is stdout or `kernel.log_path` is empty, rather than as the document's first line, so `melody:openapi:generate > openapi.json` parses unless a logger the application substituted writes to stdout; with `--out` it stays beside the `wrote` line; an unresolvable configuration reads as "not stdout" rather than panicking, and the substituted stdout logger is the application's to avoid, as the openapi document says. Responses are emitted in status order, byte-stable, and an unregistered status keeps a description.
- cache: refusals name their keys: `SetMultiple` serializes every entry before it answers and writes nothing, carrying the refused keys sorted under `keys`, the first under `key` with its cause and each key's reason under `causeByKey`, where it named whichever key the map iteration reached first; it reads a serializer's error text under a recover, as `exception` reads a foreign error's, so a panicking `Error()` of the public `Serializer` contract is answered rather than raised; `Set` and `SetMultiple` name the key in a serialization failure; `Manager.Many` leaves a corrupt entry out as it does an absent key and returns the good values beside a `DeserializationError` naming the culprits in requested order; the in-memory backend's refusal of a malformed key carries its first 128 bytes, cut on a rune boundary and marked, with its length, rather than the whole key.
- cache: `Remember`: the stored-shape round-trip runs BEFORE the computed value is stored, so a value the serializer encodes but cannot decode is refused under `cache value serialization failed`, naming the key, where it was written and read back as a miss on every call, a loop no ttl ended; every caller of one stampede-protected flight owns its answer, the first the stored shape and every later one a copy of a master taken through the serializer (so the ownership holds through a `Manager`), and a callback or caller writing the value reaches no other, a value that cannot be copied being read again from the store, a computed one refused, and a bare backend that is no `StoredValueNormalizer` answering one value; a backend or serializer panic on the leader's own access is reported as `cache remember cache access panicked`, not blamed on the callback; the nil refusals of `Remember`, `NewManager` and the third-party boundaries read through the interface, and a typed-nil error from a callback, backend or serializer reads as success. The GoDoc of `NewDefaultRememberOption` and `WithWaitTimeout` says a wait timeout shorter than the callback stores nothing under a cancelable flight only when the callback honours its context.
- cache: the in-memory backend no longer holds its exclusive lock across the whole map: the expiry sweep enumerates keys under the read lock, and a read refreshes an atomic access mark under the read lock, moving its entry to the front of the recency list at most once per second per key; the single-flight waiter counter uses the atomic wrapper type, so its alignment no longer depends on the struct layout.
- logging: **Behavioural change**: the json record's timestamp is written to a fixed width in UTC, so the stamps sort as text into the order they were written, where `RFC3339Nano` trimmed trailing zeros and a record on a whole second sorted after the fractional ones of that second.
- logging: **Behavioural change**: `LogError` anchors the record on the error the caller handed over.
- logging: **Behavioural change**: `LogOnRecoverAndExit` and `LogOnRecoverAndExitAfter` refuse an exit code outside 1..255 with a panic naming the rule — the rule `exception.NewExitError` enforces, applied to the code the handler itself would exit with.
- logging: a record is written whatever its context holds: a context closing on itself, directly or through a slice or a struct field, is rendered with `<cycle>` by the text logger, the two fallbacks of `LogError` and the exit path, and by the json fallback, now spelled key by key, instead of a fatal stack overflow; a value whose `Error()`, `Error` or `MarshalJSON` panics is named as panicked, by `LogOnRecover` and the exit handler too, instead of raising a second panic past the recovery; a defined map or slice type keeps its own `MarshalJSON` or `MarshalText`, an error that implements `json.Marshaler` is encoded rather than flattened, and a nested error renders as its message at any depth, the depth floor included; an `errors.Join` carries every branch, where `errors.Unwrap` answered none; a typed nil no longer panics the error path; `LogError` reads the mark `exception.MarkLogged` writes at the nearest `AlreadyLogged` implementer; the caller's cause keys survive; the marshal fallback keeps the context as text.
- logging: text of unknown origin reaching a plain-text line, the last line of a fatal exit and a panicking exit step included, is escaped, C1 (`U+0080`–`U+009F`) and `U+2028`/`U+2029` with C0 and DEL, so an escape sequence can no longer repaint the operator's terminal.
- logging: the json logger encodes the context — the application's own `MarshalJSON` or `String`, which may log — above its write lock, echoes a failed write to stderr once and outside the lock, and releases the lock through a deferred unlock, so a slow encoder, a stderr pipe nobody reads or a writer that panics no longer parks every logger and `Close`; it takes the write lock to close its writer and drops later writes; `jsonLogger.Close` recognizes the console by identity, `os.Stdout` and `os.Stderr`, not by file name; a rotation whose replaced descriptor refused to close is not announced as failed.
- logging: the exit handler: each step — the resolve, the record, the before-exit teardown — runs under its own shield and is abandoned past the budget `LogOnRecoverAndExitAfter` is handed, ten seconds when none is declared, a budget of `0` on the panic path taking that constant where it left the process hung before `os.Exit`; a step that panicked is no longer reported as finished, which let a clean shutdown exit 0; the final stderr echo is bounded; one emergency certificate, "process exiting after unrecovered error", is always written; a recovered `*exception.ExitError` with an out-of-range code is not honoured, a typed-nil one is a plain panic value and `LogOnRecoverAndExit` survives one carrying no error; `LogOnRecover` logs that wrapper as an anomaly and marks an error logged whether or not it panics again; the recover helpers capture the stack of the panic in flight.
- logging: `NewStandardErrorLogger` files net/http's `http: panic serving` line at error, the others staying warnings; `NewJsonLoggerWithLabels`, `NewLoggingConfiguration`, `LoggingConfiguration.LevelLabels` and `NewDefaultLoggerWithLabels` copy the label map; `LoggerFromRuntime` records the resolution it cannot serve; a typed-nil logging configuration is refused where it is registered; `EnsureLogger` replaces a typed-nil logger with the no-op one; the correlation decorator writes the real id under its key unconditionally.
- container: **Behavioural change**: `GetByType` refuses once the teardown finished, as `Get` does. Its memoized read answered the instance the teardown had already closed, where `Get` answers the container-is-closed error. A value a provider finishes building after `Close` began is still closed rather than leaked, and the failure of that close now joins the refusal, where it was dropped.
- container: `Lazy` and `LazyService` handles: a ring closed through a `Lazy` handle's late edge no longer makes a clean teardown report `container close dependency cycle detected` from `Close` or a scope's `scope.dependencyCycle`, while a ring of provider-written edges or of `WithTeardownDependency` and `WithTeardownDependencyOfType` declarations is still reported; a resolver a provider kept — the one `container.Lazy`, `lock.NewLazyLocker` and `outbox.NewLazyRepository` hold — starts a resolution chain of its own, so concurrent first uses no longer race or answer a spurious `circular service dependency detected`, and a use reaching back to a service still being built is refused as `circular service dependency detected across concurrent resolutions`; the `Lazy` GoDoc states its precondition, once the provider has returned, a use before then still possibly filed under the wrong parent or refused as circular; a `LazyService` over the container serves the service closing through it until the last `Close` returned (`IsClosed` is raised at the start), then answers `ErrContainerClosed`, one captured by a container-owned service outlives the request that built it, and one over a scope still answers `ErrScopeClosed`.
- container: the identity key of a type carries every package it is built from — a func's parameters and results (`func(alpha.Bus)` against `func(beta.Bus)`), struct fields, interface methods, and the named element of a slice, array, channel or map (`Bus`) — so two packages' same-named types register apart and a `WithTeardownDependencyOfType` declaration binds only to the type it names, as `ArmParallelTeardown` checks; `HasType` canonicalises its type as `GetByType` does; an instance override no longer poisons the type-keyed resolution of an unrelated service; the live `RegisterScoped` door refuses a type whose key another scoped type claimed; `TypesImplementing` breaks a tie between same-spelled types on their identity, `TeardownDeadlineOverrun` answers each reader its own copy, and the GoDoc of `RegisterType` says two packages' same-spelled unnamed composites collide as one name; two function-local types of one name in one package remain the one pair the key cannot tell apart, which the collision guard refuses.
- container: a value carrying only `CloseWithContext(context.Context) error`, not `Close() error`, is closed through that door by the container and by a request scope and receives the deadline, and one whose creation raced `Close`, a closing scope or an override is closed through it too, under a background context as a plain `Close()` hands the teardown, where it was skipped or leaked; a request scope closes a scoped service through `CloseWithContext` under its caller's context — declared on the concrete scope, reached by assertion as `Closed()` is, `Scope.Close()` being that door under no term — where it reached only `Close()`; a `Close` answering a typed nil reads as success, no longer as a failed close nor as `assignment to entry in nil map`, which on an armed teardown ended the process, `LogContext` bounding only the keys it holds.
- container: a failed close is reported whole and bounded: the panic a service's `Close` raises is recorded with its cause and its stack; the error carries `failureDetails` beside the one-line `failures` map — the recovered value, its type, the frames and the cause chain, bounded with the cut naming itself, a close error whose `Unwrap` panics leaving a marker; the 2048-byte cut backs off to a rune boundary; the duplicate of the failure line is dropped as one entry, not by value, so a producer's own keys and the `cause` of `fmt.Errorf("%w", err)` survive; a teardown reports both of two failed evicted or replaced instances, a close that fails and cycles names the cycle's nodes, and a service that cannot be created reports its own failure.
- container: the teardown walk that infers close order from what each service holds is breadth-first, enters each pointer once at its shallowest depth and charges its node budget when an item is queued, so the order of two fields no longer decides whether a held service is found, and a wide table — 781 MB of queue once — or an inline array of scalars, interfaces or slices, each one node, no longer spends the budget before the collaborator declared after it; wherever the value came from, its own memory included, it reads pointer words and layouts only and enters no interface, slice (a handed `All()` included), string, map or `atomic.Value`, which the race detector had reported, so a collaborator held only that way keeps the order it had; an inferred edge is written only where the canonical graph — resolved, declared and expanded edges built once, a name and its type one node — offers no way back, so a pair or ring of services holding each other, an inference against a resolution, or a type declaration a second non-strict registration made ambiguous no longer makes a clean teardown report `container close dependency cycle detected`, armed or not, the expansion of `WithTeardownDependencyOfType` being the plan's, computed under the read lock of `TeardownPlan()` and never written into the graph; armed, the members of such a pair or ring close one after the other inside their wave, a declared ring in a serial wave, serial groups are formed after the drain so unrelated listeners no longer queue behind their bus, evicted override values close each in a wave of their own, and an override installed through `OverrideProtectedInstance` records what it holds; the records are released after the teardown and keep their targets alive until then, and the plan door no longer says a closed container has nothing left to `Close`; on every path, two zero-size services sharing an address (`struct{}` pointers) or a pointer aliasing another service's first field are each closed, a scope folds the name and type filings of one instance into one node, a service closes before what it resolved through a resolver it kept, a scoped dependency the scope holds is depended on as hard as one it builds, and a scoped parent writes no edge into the container's graph.
- container: teardown declarations and arming: `WithTeardownDependencyOfType[T]()` orders the one service `T` names, no longer every service under it, so it cannot invent a ring that made a clean close report `container close dependency cycle detected` on the default path; a type several services share is ambiguous, refused by `ArmParallelTeardown` with `ErrTeardownDependencyTypeIsAmbiguous` and dropped on the default path, as `WithTeardownDependency` drops a dependency nothing registered; the arming guard holds for every later registration; a declared dependency on a scoped service, by name or by type, is refused with `ErrTeardownDependencyIsScoped`, where the type form answered `ErrTeardownDependencyWasNeverRegistered`; `ArmParallelTeardown` refuses a closed container with the cause `Register` answers; `RegisterScoped`, on the container and on a live scope, refuses `WithTeardownDependencyOfType[T]()` as it refuses `WithTeardownDependency`, with `ErrScopedTeardownDependencyUnsupported`, and the live scope honours `WithCollectionPriority`; the walk's memo of an override-evicted value goes with the value instead of living until `Close`.
- container: resolutions and scopes: a waiter coalesced behind a scoped creation and woken after the scope closed is refused `scope is closed` with `ErrScopeClosed`, where it answered `service was not available after creation finished`; a collection gathered through the resolver a provider was handed, as `AllImplementing` advises, refuses a closed scope instead of answering an empty set; a resolution after the teardown finished is refused; a value built while its scope closed is closed best-effort before the resolution fails; a request scope closes the services it built, and a service whose creation read a request scope's entry stays in that scope; a provider that panics no longer blinds the resolution around it to its scope; a finished creation drops its waiters' wait-graph edges under the lock that wakes them, and their wrapper inherits the already-logged mark; an override installed while the provider ran wins the slot, and one replacing a built instance no longer leaks it; a resolution with nothing to write takes the read lock.
- container: `FromResolver` and `FromResolverByType` refuse a nil resolver naming the service or the type, pass a melody error back whole with the name or type added to its context and wrap a foreign error naming them; `MustGet` and `MustGetByType` panic with that failure; a typed-nil error from an outside resolver, or from a provider declared with a concrete error type, reads as success, and a provider panicking with a typed-nil error no longer takes the process down; `Replacing()`'s documentation says what the code does.
- session: **Behavioural change**: `Session.Clear` ends the session and the ending latches. `RegenerateSession` clears the session it rotated away through the same latch
- session: **Behavioural change**: `NewManager` refuses a negative ttl.
- session: the file storage: `NewFileStorageFromFile` refuses a handle opened read-only (`session storage file is opened read-only`, read with `fcntl` on every Unix through `golang.org/x/sys/unix`, a new direct dependency; elsewhere the first save reports it) or with `O_APPEND`; a JSON `null` snapshot is refused at construction where the first `Save` panicked, and a basename over 200 bytes names its temp files after its digest; a `WriteAt` torn part way, or a flush failing after the new document is on disk, keeps the new in-memory state rather than rolling it back, even when the write left the document incomplete; the atomic save fsyncs the directory after the rename, `NewFileStorageFromPath` sweeps the `<name>.*.tmp` orphans a kill left between `CreateTemp` and the rename, and `FileStorage.Load` keeps its answer when its purging flush fails; the tests type-check off Unix, `go vet ./session/` clean there, the `RLIMIT_FSIZE` write probe answering one token per exit from a directory of its own.
- session: values: `Session.Set` stores a copy at the depth `Get`, `All` and `Snapshot` copy at, where the caller's map raced the response path into a fatal concurrent map write, and `Session.All` and `Session.Get` copy all the way down — maps and slices descended, a pointer, a struct, a channel or a func kept shared, as the GoDoc and SESSION.md say; the response path and `Manager.SaveSession` decide from one atomic `Snapshot`; the session carries one cleared flag, `Clear` and `Set` no longer writing two.
- session: the manager: rotating a session no longer expires the cookie of the session the rotation issued, a refused save no longer reading as a logout; `Manager.DeleteSession` buries the tombstone only after the storage removal succeeded, as `RegenerateSession` and its `Clear` already assumed, and a session deleted mid-request cannot be written back; `Manager.SaveSession` refuses a typed-nil session and holds the id to the load and delete standard; `NewManager` and its siblings refuse a positive ttl under one second, zero meaning no expiry, as `MELODY_HTTP_SESSION_TTL` does; `InMemoryStorage` refuses `Load`, `Save`, `Delete` and `Clear` after `Close`, as `FileStorage` does, and reads `now >= ExpiresAt` as lapsed; the manager's refusals name a session by a truncated SHA-256.
- security: **Behavioural change**: two firewalls declared under one name are refused, by the builder at the second declaration and by `Compile` (`security firewall name is already declared`); the registry answered the first of them and the manager the last.
- security: **Behavioural change**: a refusal on the access-control and resolution paths writes a fail-closed response instead of a nil the kernel reads as "no decision" and proceeds past.
- security: the configuration: a nil or typed-nil rule is refused by the builder, `Compile` and `NewCompiledFirewall`, naming the firewall and index (`security firewall rule is nil`), where `NewFirewall` panicked on every request; `NewApiKeyHeaderRule` refuses a nil matcher, and a typed nil is refused at both walls; the firewall, authenticator manager, access decision manager, compiled configuration and builder copy the caller's slice in, as `Rules()`/`Voters()`/`Firewalls()` copy out, so no held reference changes a live decision; an absent firewall dependency reads `SourceNone`, not `SourceFirewall`.
- security: a may-skip listener that FAILS no longer suppresses the required access-control listener's refusal, where its response was served unchecked, and a dispatcher that cannot mark required listeners, disarming that guarantee, is named at boot.
- security: tokens: `NewJwtTokenValidatorWithRevocationEpoch` reads a fractional `iat` rounded down, so `iat` 999.5 no longer passes a boundary drawn at 999.7; `newJwtTokenValidator` refuses a negative `RevocationEpochSkew` and copies the secret; `ImpersonationToken.OnBehalfOf` carries a nested impersonation's chain, `b` behind `a` behind the admin, where the admin was dropped, an impersonator whose originating actor names somebody else keeping its own identity; `InMemoryTokenStore` keeps unknown `Claims` members and stamps `IssuedAt` inside the insert's critical section on `Put` and `PutWithTtl`.
- security: failure records: a token-source resolution failure is filed once, with method and path, a sub-500 refusal (an expired jwt, a malformed Authorization) at warning; an infrastructure failure is filed at Error, marked by the framework's validators or by an application's through `MarkInfrastructureFailure`, an unmarked one at Info; a login, logout or authenticator failure whose event dispatch also fails keeps the authentication error as cause and names the broken listener, where it read "event listener returned error".
- security: typed nils: `SecurityContextSetOnRuntime` and `SecurityContextFromRuntime` guard with `IsNilInterface` and log through `LoggerFromRuntime`, so a typed-nil `Scope()` or a closed scope reached from `IsGranted` answers `(nil, false)`; `RoleVoter`, `RoleHierarchyVoter`, `PathPrefixMatcher.Matches`, `ApiKeyHeaderRule.Check`, `FirewallRegistry.Match` and both kernel listeners read the token or request through the interface, so a nil pointer, whose `IsAuthenticated` answers true before `Roles()` dereferences it, no longer panics on the access path, as `ResolverTokenSource.Resolve` already read it.
- lock: **Behavioural change**: `RunExclusive` and `LeaderGate` cancel the callback's runtime at the lease's demotion instant — half a renewal cadence, a quarter of the ttl by default, before the lease last written lapses — when no renewal has landed, where an unanswering or failing store demoted the caller only at the lapse and the next leader's `OnElected` ran before the first was told to stop; each renewal is bounded to that instant on its own goroutine and a failed one retried once, a callback done by then is a clean run, and a later renewal no longer saves the lease.
- lock: **Behavioural change**: a `LeaderGate` starts renewing its lease before `OnElected` runs, and the hook receives a runtime whose context is cancelled when the lease is lost.
- lock: the lease clock: `RunExclusive` dates the lease from when the acquire or renewal was ISSUED and demotes on that clock, not one renewal's outcome, so an unanswering renewal cannot leave two holders, and a success landing past the demotion instant still demotes; the gate's deadline is monotonic, session mode (a non-positive ttl) has no lease clock, and `IsLeader` turns false the moment the lease is lost; a panicking backend `Refresh` is the lost lease's cause with its stack, and a gate whose `OnElected` panicked with no `OnLost` journals the lost term naming the hook once.
- lock: panics and records: a `LeaderGate` whose `OnElected` panics ends its term, releases the lock, hands the panic and stack to `OnLost` or the journal and campaigns again after `RetryInterval`, where it stayed a false leader with `IsLeader` true; a panic in `OnLost` or `OnCampaignError` ends nothing, the hooks and refresh goroutines are shielded, and without those hooks each failed campaign or lost term is journaled; a panicking `RunExclusive` callback no longer has `Release` go out on the same `Lock` that `Refresh` is using, the panic still propagating unchanged to the cli layer; `RunExclusive` and `LeaderGate.Run` refuse a typed-nil scope or container before locking, where `runtime.New` refused it in the release and the lock stayed to its ttl, as a custom `Runtime` could carry; a failed release is logged with the lock name and the cause, `ExclusiveCommand` tells a shutdown from contention, and a callback failing beside a lost lease joins the cause chain.
- container, http, cache, security, exception: a recovered panic value whose own rendering panics, which `%v` and `fmt`'s containment of a panicking `Error` or `String` let through, no longer raises a second panic out of the recovery and, under `ArmParallelTeardown`, the process; each recovery names such a value by its type.
- exception, logging, application: a failure whose error panics in `Unwrap`, `As` or its logged mark no longer ends the process from the reader filing it: `LogContext`, the three `FromError` doors, `MarkLogged`, `IsAlreadyLogged`, `Logged`, `BuildCauseChain`, `BuildCauseContextChain`, `AsHttpException` and `logging.LogError` search the chain under a recover, as `Error` and `Context` already were, the walk stopping at a panicking `Unwrap` with `the links below could not be read, their Unwrap panicked: …`, and a panicking mark reads as not logged; it killed the async audit storage of `bunorm` and stood behind the websocket `OnMessage` recovery, the consumer and the shutdown hook, whose error is now built through `FromError`; a delegate RETURNING such an error is dead-lettered, and a recovered value whose `Unwrap` panics resolves at the exit handler, under its own message.
- openapi, wiring: `melody:openapi:generate` and `melody:wiring:generate` declare `--quiet`, true by default as under `StandardFlags`, so `melody:openapi:generate > openapi.json` no longer starts with the banner, `--quiet=false` restoring it, and `--no-color`; their journal door reads a configuration with no kernel section as "not stdout"; both write through a temp file named `.melody-artifact-*.tmp` and a rename, and refuse to replace a file that is not theirs, for openapi and the route manifest anything but an empty file or one valid JSON document.
- container, debug: a ring the teardown drain cannot open is closed as one unit and the drain continues, a pure dependency of a ring member closed after the ring as its edge proves, where it was closed first and named in the close error's `nodes`, the `container.dependencyCycle` and `scope.dependencyCycle` failures and the view as `proved`; only ring members are named, in `TeardownPlanEntry.Cycle` too, and two rings make two serial waves; `TeardownPlanEntry` carries `SerialGroup`, printed by `debug:container` as the `group` column and json key; a scoped registration sharing a built service's name carries no teardown item, where it showed a `lifetime: scoped` row with `ordering: none`.
- container, application: a teardown past its deadline records who spent it, where an overrun under twice the budget exited 0 with no trace: the budget, the time spent, the closers running at the deadline (`spentBy`), those reached after it (`starved`) and each close's duration, as the `deadline` context of a failed close's error or through `TeardownDeadlineOverrun() exceptioncontract.Context`, a read-only door asserted like `IsClosed`, which the application journals as a warning after a clean close; an overrun alone never fails the `Close()` nor changes the exit code.
- logging, cli: the control-character escaping renders a byte that is not valid UTF-8 as `\xNN`, so the C1 introducer `0x9b` becomes `\x9b` as promised, and the json document of `--format=json` and every json logger record spell the C1 block as `\u00NN`, which `encoding/json` emits raw, so neither repaints a terminal nor breaks a record on a NEL.
- http/static: **Behavioural change**: an explicit cache max age of zero is honoured as `Cache-Control: public, max-age=0` — always revalidate, with the ETag and Last-Modified machinery intact — because the configuration door validates zero as a distinct choice.
- http/static: the filesystem mode serves a public directory spelled `.` or `/`, the containment reading the relative path between the two made absolute rather than a textual prefix, so a symlink with an absolute target inside a relative directory is served and one leading out stays refused; the file server refuses a spelling `path.Clean` folds into the mount root — `/..`, `/.`, `//`, `/open/..` —, consults its excluded paths against the index file the root resolves to, and records its two ordinary exits, a non-retrieval method and a path outside the prefix, at debug; `NewFileServer` copies the configuration, struct and both lists; an embedded server proves its public directory at construction, refusing by name an absent one or a file, and derives no cache validators from a modification time it lacks; the entity tag reads the modification time at nanosecond resolution.
- storage: `LocalStorage.Put` keeps its temp objects in a reserved hidden namespace beside the key, `.melody-storage-<sha256 of the leaf>.<16 hex>.tmp`, so a key whose leaf fills the 255 bytes of a path component is stored, and a later `Put` sweeps that key's abandoned temp objects of exactly that shape, an ordinary key like `report.tmp-0123456789abcdef` never removed, reading the directory in batches of 256 names, the GoDoc stating one pass per `Put`; a temp object a crash left under the naming of `v3.13.0`, `<leaf>.tmp-<16 hex>`, is never swept and is removed by hand; the copy failure says "could not copy the payload into the storage object".
- debug: **Behavioural change**: `debug:router`, `debug:events`, `debug:parameters` and `debug:middleware` apply `--limit` and `--offset` to the items they render instead of only reporting them in the payload.
- debug: error contexts: `debug:container` reports WHY a service failed to build, the `caused by` lines rendered at every verbosity of the `--build` list, the ladder cutting the message and context but not the causes; the context is read through the `ContextProvider` contract rather than `*exception.Error` alone, from the first link of the chain carrying one, nine links deep, so an `HttpException` or a userland error contributes its context, where it rendered `{}`, and a panicking `Context()` is rendered as a `contextPanicked` entry; a defined map or slice type is rendered through its `MarshalJSON` or `MarshalText` and walked, `<marshal failed>` when the method fails or panics; the cause chains of `debug:container --build` and `debug:middleware` are walked from the failure itself rather than a bare `errors.Unwrap`; the redaction sees the DEFINED type; the walk is bounded in depth with its cycle guard scoped to the current path, a self-referring context rendered with the loop marked; the context json is cut to the cell width only in the table format.
- debug: the teardown view of `debug:container`: `debug:container --build` resolves each registration under its own lifetime and carries a `lifetime` key, so a scoped registration sharing a built container service's name is two rows, the container one keeping its `teardown` key; the teardown block of `debug:container` renders on the `--build` sweep and the single-service door, is windowed on the container services shown by `--limit`/`--offset`, where a `--limit=1` listing rendered every node, and the json document carries it beside each built service; its `ordering` column reads the edges on both sides and gains a `closed after` column naming the dependents, so a pure dependency no longer reads `none`, which is now kept for a node with no relation; the summary orders its segments `total | shown | ok | error`.
- debug: `debug:events` counts distinct subscriber types across the whole dispatcher under a `subscriber types` column, where `subscribers` read as installations, two instances of one type counting once; its verbose listener detail follows the window and the direction; it is registered with the declaration channel, so a console dispatcher renders the security pair the serving process wires.
- debug: a nil secret parameter renders as `(empty)` instead of the mask; `NewMiddlewareCommand` refuses a nil provider by panic and `Run` renders the envelope with the named refusal of a zero-value command before failing with its exit-coded error, where it wrote zero bytes; a route attribute that cannot be represented no longer takes the process down, and the `debug:router` comparator is total.
- messagebus: **Behavioural change**: `melody:messagebus:consume` separates the shutdown signal from the handler lifetime — the signal stops the pull of new deliveries, while in-flight handlers and their `Ack`/`Nack` keep a live context for the whole `WithShutdownGrace` window.
- messagebus: transports: `RegisterTransports` refuses a nil or typed-nil transport at registration, naming it, as the `amqp` module did, where the consume command dereferenced it; `TransportsCloser.Close` contains each `Close()` on its own, a transport whose `Close` panics included, a nil entry named as nil, read through the typed-nil door a plain `nil ==` lets through, and a typed-nil close result read as a close, the loop now running on every process rather than only under `melody:messagebus:consume`, so one bad transport no longer abandons the teardown of every transport sorted after it, whose broker connections lived as long as the process; `NewInMemoryTransport` refuses a negative buffer size in the framed form, not a raw `make(chan)` panic.
- messagebus: the in-memory requeue: a delayed requeue is journaled when dropped, through the logger of the runtime `Nack` was called with, and when the transport closed during its delay, where every delayed `Nack` without a logger wrote an emergency line; a transport of size 0 requeues a nacked message on a goroutine waiting for the next receive or the close, where the requeue was refused; the consumer replaces the redelivery, delay and dead-letter attempt stamps instead of appending one per cycle, an `Envelope` implemented outside the package still getting one appended.
- messagebus: an overlapping `Run` of the auto-registered consume command no longer races itself; its error records fall back to the emergency logger when the runtime resolves none; a dispatch that succeeded while nothing sent, handled or received the message is logged; the retry-delay guard computes in `int64` rather than `int`, where the default cap over a microsecond `BaseDelay` wrapped negative on a 32-bit build into a full `MaxDelay` first wait.
- wiring: **Behavioural change**: a malformed exclude pattern is refused before anything is walked, and an exclude that matched no constructor is reported — and fails `--strict` — the way an unused bind is.
- wiring: **Behavioural change**: an unknown `//melody:` directive is a hard error naming the file, the line and the text, instead of being silently ignored.
- wiring: **Behavioural change**: a directive with a blank after its slashes, `// melody:ignore`, is refused naming the line and the expected spelling, where it read as prose and failed open (an ignored constructor registered, a scoped one made a singleton); an `--out` reaching a scanned directory through a symlink, or a directory scanned through one, is refused as inside it.
- wiring: `melody:wiring:generate` in stdout mode prints the source alone and journals its report — `registered N constructors`, skipped and excluded files, unused binds and excludes — through the application's logger, or the emergency one when the journal is stdout, where it preceded the `package` clause and `melody:wiring:generate > wiring_gen.go` never compiled; with `--out` it stays beside `wiring written to`; counts are information, lost coverage a warning, and the global binds' reach one warning record.
- wiring: a `//melody:service` naming no constant, a `//melody:bind` with no assignment, no equals sign or an empty half are refused at the site, where they failed open; `//melody:ignore` takes a trailing reason, `//melody:ignore kept as a test double`.
- wiring: the generator models a registration's whole identity, `T` and `*T` under one name as the container does, so two constructors registering one service — two `New*` of one type, two of one service constant, a `func() Foo` beside a `func() *Foo` — are refused naming both sites, where the generated file panicked at the second `Register`; a scoped constructor shadowing a container name is emitted with `Replacing()`.
- wiring: an `--out` inside a scanned package directory is refused, the guard comparing path components, so `..hidden` is inside, and refusing an unrelatable path; an empty import path or directory on a binding is refused; a symlinked package directory is resolved before the walk; `--strict` reports every violation at once; the generator says when it cannot check the bind targets.
- internal: `SplitOutsideQuotes` answers a second result saying the member cap cut the value, where the remainder was one member every reader dropped though it could carry a refusal, `gzip;q=0` as the sixty-fifth member, and the client got what it had refused; `EscapeControlCharacters` and `EscapeJsonC1Block` spell the escapes by table, the text byte for byte the same, and `RuneDisplayWidth` answers one for an ASCII rune before its table probes.
- internal: the deep-copy memo is keyed on the static type beside the pointer and the length, so a defined type over `map[string]any` or `[]any` (a claims map, a `Meta`) no longer shares a key with its plain value, which made `Session.Get`/`Set`/`All`, the token store's claims and the route attributes panic intermittently; `RedactQueryValuesForDiagnostics` redacts each pair in place, in order, keeping repeated names, instead of re-encoding through `url.Values`, so the internal-auth refusal no longer shows two identical strings and the access log keeps the query sent, a pair whose name does not decode still redacting the query whole, a pair with no `=` (`?9f8a7b3c`) becoming the marker whole and an empty segment, a trailing or doubled `&`, staying empty; `WriteFileAtomically`, behind `melody:openapi:generate --out` and the route-manifest command, flushes the temp file and fsyncs the directory before reporting success, as the `migrate` and `cron` writers do, keeps the destination's mode, `0644` only for a new file, where it widened a `0600` document, and replaces `wiring:generate`'s own copy.
- bag: **Behavioural change**: a key present with a nil value reports as unset from `String`, `StringSlice`, `StringStrict` and `StringSliceStrict`, matching what `Int`, `Bool`, `Float64` and `Duration` already reported for the same state.
- bag: `bag.String` on a repeated request key answers its FIRST value, as `Request.Input` and `url.Values.Get` do, where it panicked through `bag.StringOrDefault` and `bag.HasNonEmptyString`, so one duplicated key (`?name=a&name=b`) was a 500 any client could raise, the whole list still read with `StringSlice`/`StringAt`; `Int`, `Bool` and `Float64` read a repeated key's first value too, as `String` does, where they refused its `[]string`, an empty list reading as unset; a present non-string scalar read as a string reports ABSENT, so `StringOrDefault` takes the default and `HasNonEmptyString` answers false; `ParameterBag.Set` allocates the map the zero value lacks, so `ParameterBag{}` accepts a write; `ParameterBag.Has` reads under the lock without the copy `Get` pays.
- validation: **Behavioural change**: the parsed validate tag and the constraint a parameterized rule resolves to are memoized instead of being rebuilt for every value the validator reaches.
- validation: the walk: the cycle set is scoped to the current descent path, only an ancestor still on it closing a cycle, and a pointer several fields share is walked once per depth through a memo keyed on the pointer, its type and the depth, its errors re-spelled under every path that reaches it, where a value built by the application cost 2^N through `Validate`; an error a constraint raises under a field or type of its own stays verbatim under every path, "under the walked path" read in the walk's grammar so `BillingLine` is no longer read under `Billing` nor recalled as `ShippingLine`, and that one shape is walked per path, as the validation document says; a field promoted through stacked embeds below a diamond is validated again, and a struct whose promoted json codec is `time.Time`'s is not walked.
- validation: constraints: `NewRegex("")` keeps the empty pattern as the constraint's error, so `Validate` refuses every non-empty value with `invalidPattern` and `Error` says why, where it matched everything, the tag door (`regex=`) refusing it already; `greaterThan` and `lessThan` judge a float field against the declared bound exactly; a typed-nil error from a custom constraint reads as success; `createConstraintWithParams` refuses a name the registry cannot answer; the reason a parameterized constraint refused its parameters travels in the error context, the parser naming the segment it could not read.
- validation: the time-codec, parsed-tag and constructed-constraint memos settle on one verdict and one instance under a concurrent first touch, and `ToExceptionError` copies the internal context map into the exception.
- serializer: negotiation gives each media type the quality of the most specific range covering it, so an exact range wins over a wildcard whatever the order and a `q=0` range refuses that type; an `Accept` header the member cap cut is refused as not acceptable (`ErrNotAcceptable`, a 406), where it was scored on the members before the cut, and one with no readable member, `","` or every `q` malformed, gets the default representation without the error recorded at warning on every such request; `PlainTextSerializer` copies on both directions, a typed-nil deserialization target is refused as the untyped nil is, and the two soft resolvers report through `logging.LoggerFromRuntime` rather than the Must door.
- translation: **Behavioural change**: `NewManager` keeps every catalog of a locale, in the order given, and a lookup asks them in that order until one answers, so an application that relied on the last catalog replacing the earlier ones now gets the earlier ones answering first, where every message living only in the first answered its raw id; a catalog whose `Locale()` is empty is refused as the nil one is. See `UPGRADE.md`.
- translation: **Behavioural change**: a `.json` file in the translations directory whose name does not parse as `<domain>.<locale>.json` is a hard error naming the file.
- translation: `NewManager` refuses a nil catalog, where skipping it answered raw ids for a whole locale, and the empty domain resolves to the default at the manager's door, so a catalog answering the asked-for domain verbatim is asked for `messages`.
- exception: **Behavioural change**: `NewHttpException` and `NewHttpExceptionWithCause` refuse an informational status, admitting `[200, 599]`: an exception raised with 103 was served as a 200 carrying the error body, and a program constructing a 1xx exception now panics at construction, a 1xx answer being written as a response; a status above 599, which net/http's `WriteHeader` panics on deep in the response path, is refused at construction too.
- exception: nil receivers and panicking errors: `Error.Unwrap`, `HttpException.Unwrap` and `ExitError.Unwrap` answer nil on a nil receiver, `ExitError.Error`, `Error.Error` and `HttpException.Error` a placeholder, and every other accessor — `Message`, `Context`, `SetContext`, `SetContextValue`, `CauseErr`, `Level`/`StatusCode`, `AlreadyLogged`, `MarkAsLogged` — answers it too, as every accessor of `ExitError` does, so the typed nil `FromError(nil)` produces as a cause no longer panics `errors.Join`, nor `errors.Is` and `errors.As`, which call `Unwrap` on every link, nor `AsHttpException` inside the kernel's recovery; `FromError`, `FromErrorWithLevel`, `FromErrorWithLevelAndContext` and `Logged` render the message under the recover `LogContext` uses, and `LogContext` and the cause chain render text through `renderErrorText`, so an `Error()` or a foreign `Context()` that panics no longer unwinds past the 500 and the `kernel.exception` dispatch; `LogContext` anchors the walk on the top error's own wrap links rather than the nearest `*Error` a deep search found, keeping every link's context, and walks both unwrap shapes, a joined error's branches side by side; the context and logged mark of `Error` and `HttpException` are guarded by a lock; `MarkLogged` marks the nearest `AlreadyLogged` implementer and answers a typed nil unchanged; `NewExitError` refuses a code outside `[1, 255]`.










- application: **Behavioural change**: a route queued from inside a module boot hook is refused.
- application: **Behavioural change**: a module registered from inside a module boot hook is refused.
- application: **Behavioural change**: `RegisterConfiguration` refuses any name but `logging`.
- application: **Behavioural change**: an http process whose `.env` artifacts contributed no keys at all refuses to boot.
- application: **Behavioural change**: a middleware factory that yields nil, or a typed nil, fails the pipeline build naming the definition.
- application: **Behavioural change**: a teardown failure on Run's normal return exits non-zero.
- application: **Behavioural change**: a relative `MELODY_LOG_PATH` is anchored to the project directory, through the helper the logs and cache directories already use, and the file's parent directory is created before the open.
- application: the kernel's default listeners are wired at the end of `Boot` in every process shape, and the framework exception listener among them decides at each `kernel.exception` dispatch whether an error handler is installed, standing aside for it, so a handler installed after `Boot` is consulted, an application serving the kernel through its own `net/http.Server` without `Run` keeps the framework rendering of an `HttpException`, and a refusal the firewall or access control dispatch from `kernel.request` is rendered by the framework listener instead of a 500 journaled nowhere.
- application: shutdown and teardown: the message bus transports are closed by every process, not only one resolving `messagebus.ServiceTransports`, where an http process kept the broker connection alive against the `Close() error` contract, and they close after the buses under `ServiceBus` and `ServiceConsumeBus`, with nothing to declare; an http shutdown whose serve or server-shutdown phase failed still drains the open request scopes, a hijacked connection the server's own `Shutdown` cannot drain included, every failing phase contributing its cause, and a panicking shutdown hook is logged while the drain continues; two racing closes report the teardown failure once; a listen failure in the instant a shutdown begins is reported, and the failure the process exits on is rendered once; the record explaining a dying process is written before the teardown, through a logger that still writes or to the configured destination, a boot dying after the container was built closes it between the record and the exit, and the exit handler survives an application assembled without `NewApplication`; the container logger closes its log file when arming the rotation watcher fails.
- application: boot: the application's routes register before any module's, each boot hook runs across every module before the next begins, one module instance reached through two providers boots once — a module held by value with an `any` field is keyed by its value, so `RegisterModule` no longer panics with `hash of unhashable type` — and a provider that is itself a module boots as that module; the logger, url generator, serializer manager, default serializer and validator can be substituted, `service.serializer` is registered, the logger is resolved once at boot after the module configuration is read, and the firewall manager is registered in every mode; a cli command name is judged trimmed, a typed nil is refused at the module, provider and command doors, and a typed-nil embedded `fs.FS` by name; `go run`'s build directory is recognised by a whole `go-build` segment; a configuration that fails to build is reported as failing to initialize, and a typed-nil `*ExitError` in a cause chain is no exit; `debug:version` leaves the application slot to the application; a middleware's function name is captured at registration; the cli run takes its instants from the kernel's clock rather than `time.Now()`.
- event: failures and nils: the already-logged mark of a panic value is read at the depth it is written, so a listener panicking with an error that wraps a logged one files no second record; the value a listener panicked with is the cause of the fabricated error, a listener's failure travels unmarked so the caller's record is the one written, and an `*exception.ExitError` passes both recovery handlers to the process boundary with its exit code; the zero value of `RequiredListenerSkippedError` answers `Error()` and `Unwrap()`, a real nil link, instead of dereferencing; a typed-nil event is refused through the interface at the dispatch door and in the recovery handler, a typed-nil listener error reads as success, and a subscriber is refused at the identity door before `SubscribedEvents()` is called.
- event: the dispatcher adapter: the `EventDispatcherAdapter` hands a listener the ORIGINAL event, so a custom type stays assertable and a written field reaches the caller, and `NewEventFromEvent` carries the stopped propagation; `EventDispatcherAdapter.RemoveListener` scrubs its bookkeeping whether or not the wrapped dispatcher held the listener; a whole subscriber installation is serialized against another, as the `subscriberMutex` comment says, so an interleaved removal cannot take down half of it; two distinct subscribers whose types carry no fields no longer collapse onto one registration; the inspector breaks equal priorities by the listener id, as the dispatch does; the level is asked once per dispatch and a listener's name resolved where it is used.
- httpclient: requests and their errors: the errors of a failed exchange keep net/http's `*url.Error` in the chain with the sanitized url, so `errors.As(err, &urlErr)` holds as `Client.Do` documents, a link whose `Err` is nil rebuilt sanitized too, and no url carried into an error keeps its secret, the sanitizer cutting the userinfo wherever the reference carries one; a client with a base url refuses a target leaving that origin, judged on the RESOLVED url; the query reaches the server as written, an option key's pair replaced and every other pair kept byte for byte, where merging through `url.Values` dropped a bare `;` or a bad escape and re-encoded the rest; a `[]byte` body is copied, not aliased.
- httpclient: options and the stream: a basic credential travels whenever asked for, empty halves included, applied after every header door so it wins over an `Authorization` header from the configuration or `WithHeader`, as the client document and GoDoc say; an invalid response-body cap is refused before dialling on both paths, a negative per-request timeout bounds a stream, a nil `RequestOption` and a nil configuration to `NewHttpClient` are refused; `StreamResponse.Body` after `Close` answers a reader that fails on the first read; `Response.Headers`/`Body` state their single-owner contract, `contract.StreamResponse` the close obligation and `contract.HttpClient.RequestStream` that the caller closes on every path.
- config: **Behavioural change**: a `${...}` reference in an `.env` file resolves against every file the environment loaded, and an undefined, self- or mutually-referencing one fails the boot.
- config: templates and resolution: a runtime parameter registered before boot with a template refuses to be read until the boot pass resolves it, where a reader got the raw `%env(...)%`, and one registered between construction and boot keeps its template for that pass; a template is decided by the resolution's grammar — a doubled percent, an `%env(...)%`, a `%name%` reference, a name-shaped run a percent opened — so `Coverage 95%` and `50% off` read before boot; a registration after boot no longer writes back onto a referenced parameter's value, a failed registration leaves no half-made parameter, and reading a parameter no longer races the resolution; `Resolve` is refused once the application serves, `MarkServing` taking the write lock the refusal reads; the deep copy is linear in distinct nodes, where it was exponential in shared ones.
- config: secrets: `MarkSecret` after the boot resolution travels to every parameter that reads the marked key, to a fixpoint, where the dsn assembled from it printed in full in `debug:parameters`, reaches a `.env` value assembled through `${KEY}` and a reader of the `kernel.*` alias of a `MELODY_*` key, and reads a doubled percent as a literal; the error of an undefined reference inside a value that may hold a credential locates it by offset and length rather than spelling a slice of the value, a plain template still naming it.
- config: refusals: the reserved-prefix boot refusal names the same environment key every run; the three refusals `Int` raises on a float carry distinct causes and the value refused; a runtime parameter names itself in the cause of its conversion error; the failure naming the MELODY_ENV resolution window says which files a reference in it can see.





- http/cors: **Breaking**: `cors.NewService` reads a nil method or header list as the one default `DefaultService` grants — Authorization included — and keeps an empty list as the expressed preference, as the origins field does: an explicitly empty (non-nil) `AllowOrigins`, `AllowMethods` or `AllowHeaders` list denies instead of becoming the wildcard or the default.
- http/cors: **Behavioural change**: a schemeless allow entry is port-significant, where `extractOriginHost` kept `url.Hostname()`, which cuts the port, so `app.example.com` matched `https://app.example.com:9000` and any port on that host, a grant `RestrictiveService` couples with credentials.
- http/cors: `RegisterRequestListener` and the `RegisterListeners` façade arrive, and the preflight is answered ahead of the security chain; an `OPTIONS` request carrying an `Origin` but no `Access-Control-Request-Method` is no longer a preflight; `Vary: Origin` is emitted on every response; `NewService` no longer panics on a credentialed configuration deciding origins through `AllowOriginFunc`.
- http/pipeline: `Builder.Describe` lists a pipeline without running its factories (`MiddlewareDescription` carries the definition's name, function, priority and gating, `Definition.SetFunctionName` names the function, and the listing mirrors the nil-factory refusal of `Build`); a definition's constraint lists and `Report.SetInactive` keep their own copies, so one slice reused across two definitions no longer rewrites their ordering constraints.
- mailer: `SmtpTransport.Send` refuses a typed-nil runtime with the record the untyped nil gets, where it dereferenced it at the dial; the `MAIL FROM` and `RCPT TO` failures say "command failed" rather than "rejected", and the two post-acceptance quit warnings carry their cause.



- clock: `FrozenClock.Advance` is forward-only and panics on a negative duration, `TravelTo` remaining the door for moving back; the frozen ticker's `Stop` returns only after its relay goroutine exited, so no new tick is produced after it, a tick already buffered still readable as with `time.Ticker`; `ClockMustFromContainer` and `ClockMustFromResolver` refuse a nil or typed-nil argument by name; the `Ticker` and `Clock` contracts state their obligations in GoDoc.




- runtime: `runtime.New` and the runtime resolvers judge their arguments through the interface.

### Security

- example: **Behavioural change**: the sign-in door reads an `application/json` body alone and answers any other with 415, before the credentials are read and without a login failure: a urlencoded form, which a page on another site can post, needed an anti-forgery token the door never had. A client that posted the form encoding sends `{"username": …, "password": …}`.
- example: `APP_SESSION_COOKIE_SECURE=always` marks the session cookie `Secure` on every response, through the kernel's session cookie policy, for a proxy terminating tls outside the ranges the forwarded scheme is believed from, where the cookie was issued without `Secure`; empty or `from-scheme` keeps the scheme's answer, and any other value refuses the boot naming the key.
- example: **Behavioural change**: the example's credentials are configuration, and outside development the boot refuses the values `.env` commits: with the compiled-in secrets of `/secure` and `/internal`, a deployment with every key changed accepted a token signed with the public constant carrying `scope: {roles: ["ROLE_ADMIN", "ROLE_ALLOWED_TO_SWITCH"]}` and an envelope under `wms-key-1`. They are read from `APP_JWT_SECRET`, `APP_INTERNAL_AUTH_SECRET` (beside `APP_INTERNAL_AUTH_KEY_ID` and `APP_INTERNAL_AUTH_APP`) and `APP_ENCRYPT_KEYS`, a blank one no longer taking the public key; with `MELODY_ENV` other than `dev` a blank or committed value refuses by name. An `APP_ENCRYPT_KEYS` entry without its `id:`, or `id:k,<key>`, is named by position, no longer carrying the key as `keyId`.
- example: **Behavioural change**: the accounts `user`, `editor` and `admin`, whose passwords are their names, are seeded into an empty directory in development only; elsewhere the first account comes from the new `example:user:create`, which reads the password from standard input and refuses it as an argument.
- example: **Behavioural change**: enrolling a second factor again replaces it only for a request presenting a current factor of the enrollment it replaces, a TOTP code on `X-2FA-Code` or a recovery code on `X-2FA-Recovery-Code`, spent as the verification door spends them; on a session alone it answers 401 with the challenge, where a stolen session became a durable second factor of the thief's. An account that lost both is reset with the new `example:twofactor:reset`.
- example: a deleted account no longer keeps its tokens when another listener of the deletion fails: an unreachable two-factor store ended the dispatch first, so its tokens kept authenticating on `/device/*`. The release outranks every listener and revokes the account's tokens at the deletion's instant; a device token issued during the deletion is taken back; the device firewall honours a token only while its account exists, with its current roles; a failed two-factor release is journaled without failing the deletion; `example:db:reset` empties the token namespace on Redis.
- example: **Behavioural change**: an account holds at most five live sessions, where unbounded sign-ins grew every session save, the sign-in past the cap ending the oldest at the sign-in door and the firewall's login handler, through the session index the schema migration holds; a sign-out frees its place. A sign-in whose index or liveness read fails stores nothing; a row admitted in the last minute counts as live, so concurrent sign-ins cannot pass the cap, a failed save holding its place for that minute alone; without a database the index restarts from zero.
- example: **Behavioural change**: an account holds at most four event streams open at once, and the process 256, counted across `/events/stream/` and the `/ws` websocket route; a stream past either cap is refused with 429 before anything is committed and a closed stream frees its place, where one client could hold every descriptor of the process.
- http, security: the router and every reader of the routed path — the canonical guard, the access-control listener, the firewall path matcher, the internal-auth binding, the compression exclusion and the static file server — read the path as the client sent it, while the raw path still unescapes to the decoded one: `URL.EscapedPath` re-spelled `/admin%2Fusers{` with a separator in place of `%2F`, reaching the `/admin/*rest` handler, where it now routes as `/admin%2Fusers` does, the one segment `admin/users`.
- http, security: **Behavioural change**: the kernel refuses with a 400, after matching and before authorization, a path that leading or trailing whitespace would be trimmed from — `/public%20`, `/public%09`, `/public%C2%A0`, or ` /public` from a front handler such as `StripPrefix` on `/api%20/public` — where the router served the protected catch-all under `/public ` while the matcher trimmed it onto an exact `PUBLIC_ACCESS` rule of `/public`, an anonymous grant. Whitespace inside a path is not refused.
- http, security: **Behavioural change**: the access-control matcher, the firewall path matcher and the canonical-path guard read the spelling the router matched, `RequestPathAsRouted`, an encoded separator kept in its segment, not the decoded `URL.Path`: `/public%2F` and `/public%2F%2F`, the resource `public/` behind a protected catch-all, read as `/public/` and were served anonymously under the `PUBLIC_ACCESS` rule of `/public`. `/admin%2Fusers`, never routed under `/admin`, is no longer claimed by a rule for `/admin/`; a rule names such a resource with `%2F`. The static file server, its exclusion list, the compression exclusion list and the internal-auth binding read it too: `/static/private%2Fsecret.txt` served `private/secret.txt` under a protected prefix judged as `/static`, and an envelope for `/files/a/b` authenticated `/files/a%2Fb`. `v3.13.0` routed on the decoded path.
- config: a configuration failure no longer copies a value into the boot record: a godotenv parse failure is reported by shape, not by quoting the line of the `.env` it failed on, and its sanitizer reads the "unterminated quoted value" shape by its prefix before cutting at " near ", where `PASSWORD="hunter2 near the door` with its closing quote forgotten was copied up to those words; a secret parameter withholds the strconv or parse error under its conversion error, which quotes the value; a reference to a non-string parameter reports the type it found instead of the value.
- config: the refusal of an unclosed `%name` reference or an unterminated `%env(` carries the parameter and the percent's byte offset and none of the value, where `DB_PASSWORD=Pa%SSword1` put `%SSword1` in the boot record under `reference`; inside a value read through `%env(KEY)%` it also names the key once, by the innermost read, a `%parameter%` there being scanned under its own name. A bounded `%env(...)%` candidate closing with `)%` that is no placeholder keeps its echo.
- config: `MarkSecret` no longer leaves unredacted a value assembled through a parameter's `kernel.*` alias: a dsn built from a secret read through the other spelling printed in full beside the redacted key; the propagation follows every alias the parameter answers to.
- validation: **Behavioural change**: the validator bounds what one `Validate` call costs and answers, where a 1 MiB body bought a 400 above a gigabyte: a map key past 128 bytes is spelled as its first 128 bytes, cut on a rune, plus `...(truncated)`; the depth cut is one entry naming the truncated list or map, no longer each element; at most 1,000 errors, then one entry under the root field `""` with code `errorLimitExceeded` and context `maxErrors`, the 1,001 still refusing the value; a path is cut at its head past 256 bytes behind `...(truncated)`, an answer at 512 KiB with that entry carrying `maxAnswerBytes`. `NewValidatorWithLimits` configures them (see Added).
